#2156. Michael O’Neill makes revelation over Terry Devlin’s Portsmouth future which will leave Championship rivals ‘disappointed’

Portsmouth have enjoyed a fine season to date, and versatile star Terry Devlin has been a standout player for John Mousinho’s side. Mainly playing at right-back, the 23-year-old has chipped in with four goals in 14 Championship games, including a brace in the 3-1 win over Swansea City at the start of November.

Devlin’s progression has brought interest from elsewhere, but Pompey have been working to secure his future at Fratton Park. Now, Northern Ireland boss Michael O’Neill has indicated that a positive conclusion is close. Speaking on the player’s situation with BBC Sport over the international break, he said: “He’s a very valuable player for Portsmouth.

“I think he’s in the process of signing a new contract there, and I imagine a lot of teams in the Championship will be disappointed by that.”

Devlin last extended his contract in February 2025 and he is tied down until 2028. He is in his fourth season with the South Coast outfit, having joined from Glentoran in the summer of 2023.

Tying Devlin down to a new deal marks a key move from Portsmouth. It secures his future before he enters the final 18 months of his current deal, but it also serves as a statement of intent to lock down one of their standout players and most valuable assets.

The 23-year-old has ups and downs over the course of his Pompey career but as a true utility player, he is a hugely valuable figure in Mousinho’s squad. His athleticism, attitude and well-rounded skillset means he can be trusted to deliver all over the pitch.

Furthermore, the deal will allow Devlin to stay focused on his own game. It will quieten noise over his future and a potential move elsewhere as ambitious sides like Wrexham have previously looked to prise him away from Fratton Park.

At 23, Devlin has time to grow on and off the pitch. On his current trajectory, his value will only grow, so Pompey will be glad to have him tied down to a secure and longer contract.

As touched on before, Devlin has been the subject of interest from other clubs previously. Those admirers will have been keeping a close eye on his situation with 18 months left on his deal.

However, on a new long-term deal, Portsmouth will be in a better place to stand firm on their valuation. If someone wants to buy him, they will have to pay a handsome fee to do so.

It doesn’t rule out interest or bids, especially if he keeps progressing as he has done, but Devlin’s future at Portsmouth will be far more certain if a new long-term contract is in place.

#2155. Milan, Inter e Juventus, la conta degli infortunati: da Maignan a Lautaro e Conceicao, le novità

Il Milan tira un sospiro di sollievo circa le condizioni di Mike Maignan: stando a quanto riporta l'Equipe , il portiere, dopo aver abbandonato il campo ieri sera per un colpo alla coscia ed un fastidio agli adduttori, nella giornata di oggi ha svolto dei primi esami che hanno dato un esito rassicurante: dovrebbe tornare ad allenarsi a metà settimana e non saltare dunque partite con il club. Secondo il quotidiano, non dovrebbero esserci problemi per Maignan per il prossimo weekend: il portiere dovrebbe dunque essere a disposizione per Milan-Frosinone di domenica. Gli esami di cui parla il noto quotidiano francese però non sono stati svolti con il Club: domani il francese effettuerà ulteriori test con lo staff medico del Milan.

Ricominciati oggi intanto gli allenamenti a Milanello per i non convocati in nazionale dopo i sette giorni di riposo concessi da mister Ruben Amorim in concomitanza con la prima settimana della sosta nazionali. Attesa per i controlli previsti a Londra i controlli per Fikayo Tomori e Ruben Loftus-Cheek per valutare l’evoluzione delle rispettive condizioni: a seconda dell'esito dei rispettivi esami, si capirà di più sui tempi di recupero e il ritorno in campo. Secondo quanto appreso da Milannews.it, Strahinja Pavlovic non si è allenato in gruppo: il difensore serbo ha avuto un problema ai reni e oggi ha svolto un lavoro personalizzato.

Buone notizie per Luciano Spalletti, che alla ripresa degli allenamenti potrebbe ritrovare alcune pedine importanti per il reparto offensivo. Dopo tre giorni di riposo, la Juventus tornerà al lavoro domani alla Continassa, con l'attenzione rivolta soprattutto alle condizioni di Nico Gonzalez. Come scrive Il Corriere dello Sport, l'argentino è fermo dal 22 ottobre, quando aveva accusato un problema fisico durante la sfida di Europa League contro il Rennes. A distanza di quasi un mese, il suo ritorno in gruppo sembra ormai vicino e l'obiettivo è tornare tra i convocati per la partita contro il Venezia. Il recupero offrirebbe un'alternativa in più a Spalletti, che potrebbe utilizzare Gonzalez non soltanto sulla fascia destra, ma eventualmente anche come centravanti, ruolo già ricoperto dall'argentino durante l'esperienza allo Stoccarda. Segnali incoraggianti arrivano anche da Francisco Conceicao, che ha già completato una seduta di allenamento con il resto della squadra. Il portoghese si candida dunque a trovare spazio contro il Venezia, almeno a partita in corso, considerando che Kolo Muani appare favorito per partire titolare. Vicino al rientro tra i disponibili anche Andrea Cambiaso, il cui recupero permetterebbe al tecnico di avere maggiori alternative sulle corsie esterne. Nel frattempo, alla Continassa si rivedranno inizialmente i giocatori rimasti a Torino durante la sosta. I nazionali cominceranno invece a rientrare da martedì, consentendo a Spalletti di ritrovare progressivamente il gruppo e preparare la sfida di campionato. Le prossime sedute serviranno soprattutto a verificare le condizioni di Gonzalez e degli altri recuperati, con l'obiettivo di affrontare il Venezia con una rosa più completa rispetto alle ultime settimane.

Il vicepresidente dell'Inter e leggenda del club nerazzurro, Javier Zanetti, ha parlato a margine del "Premio Modello Virtuoso del Calcio Italiano", intitolato a Italo Galbiati. Ecco le sue parole a Sky Sport: "Come sta Lautaro? Lautaro veramente sta facendo, in questi ultimi periodi, le ultime prove per essere a disposizione. Credo che abbia fatto una riabilitazione con i dottori, con lo staff, sempre molto accurata. E conoscendo lui, la voglia che ha di tornare, mi auguro che possa essere già a disposizione". Intanto in casa nerazzurra si attendono notizie su Marcus Thuram: il francese potrebbe allenarsi con gli altri già domani.

#2154. Mauricio Pochettino is adored by his players, big on culture and good vibes, not afraid to crack the whip and does not suffer fools - exactly what dysfunctional Man United are crying out for

By KIERAN GILL, MAIL SPORT REPORTER and MATT BARLOW, FOOTBALL WRITER and DANIEL MATTHEWS, US SENIOR SPORTS WRITER

Published: 17:59 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 18:01 EST, 10 November 2026

After one press conference during Mauricio Pochettino’s ill-fated spell at Chelsea, he hosted an Argentinian-style BBQ for approximately 130 people, including his players and their families, under a marquee set up in the first-team car park at their training ground.

Returning to the room and the assembled throng of journalists still working, Pochettino popped a large plate of sausages and steaks down on the desk. He insisted it did not constitute bribery for better coverage of him. Just thought we might like to join.

This was Pochettino personified. Big on togetherness. Insisted on creating a homely environment for everyone. Believed a harmonious culture at Cobham would help performances on the pitch. Even kept a big box of lemons in his office because he reckoned they harnessed ‘energia universal’. He emphasised the importance of a good mood.

Goodness knows that’s what Manchester United need right now. And if they do decide to dismiss Michael Carrick, the prospect of managing possibly the biggest club in world football would not faze Pochettino – now the bookies’ favourite to take over.

Indeed, the 54-year-old would relish the challenge.

A few days before USA’s World Cup victory against Bosnia and Herzegovina to reach the last 16 at this summer’s tournament, coach Pochettino invited a small group of journalists to interview him at the team hotel – the Ritz-Carlton in Laguna Niguel, overlooking the Pacific Ocean 50 miles south of Los Angeles.

Manchester United could do with a dose of Mauricio Pochettino - and he would relish the challenge

The United States manager - who would cost around £7.5million to release - has openly admitted he wants to lead them in the future

Towards the end of the interview the Manchester United job came up in conversation. Pochettino turned to his long-time assistant coach Jesus Perez and said: ‘One day, your dream comes true... you go to Manchester United!’.

The Argentine clarified somewhat by explaining that Perez, while living in London, would often visit Manchester on days off to visit the National Football Museum. Perez responded with a smile: ‘United should have got you’.

One thing about Pochettino that Manchester United players will soon figure out if he takes the reins at Old Trafford is that he is not afraid to crack the whip and does not suffer fools. At the World Cup, he appeared to criticise the intervention of FIFA - and President Trump – in suspending Folarin Balogun’s ban to make the striker available for a knockout clash with Belgium.

‘I feel disappointed with too many people. They put politics and manipulation, talk about ethics and integrity [first]. If we talk about the history of this game, I am disappointed in a personal way.’

With Pochettino, you’re either all in or you’re out. Look back to his time at Tottenham for evidence of that: the decision to exile stars such as Younes Kaboul, Etienne Capoue and Paulinho into a ‘bomb squad’ - and give chances to stars such as Harry Kane - was the start of his success there.

He’s a builder of teams, works with the tools he has and makes players better. Again, that sounds like the sort of thing United are crying out for.

Pochettino is big on culture – he adopted the mantra ‘culture eats strategy for breakfast’ after a meeting with the chairman of U.S. fast food chain Chick-fil-A – and worked hard to change the holiday camp feel at the USA team. Senior stars believed they did not need to put in the extra work to keep their places. The most notable example saw national team poster-boy Christian Pulisic, once of Chelsea, attempt to skip key games after a long season for AC Milan. Pochettino put a stop to that.

The Argentine does not suffer fools, is big on culture, and can deal with big club executives - his time at Tottenham taught us that

He can manage upwards, too. He handled chairman Daniel Levy well, and credited open dialogue as being key to the partnership with the chairman which yielded a runners-up finish in the Premier League and a run to the Champions League final.

‘I speak to him a lot, we talk a lot, we have very good communication,’ Pochettino said in 2018.

‘That means sometimes we agree and sometimes we don't agree. But after, when the decision is made, we are so strong in the delivery of it which shows it is best for the club’.

His cordial relationship with Levy meant Spurs were unconcerned when he met Sir Alex Ferguson in 2016 at Scott’s in Mayfair towards the end of Louis van Gaal’s troubled reign at Old Trafford.

There was nothing untoward in that - after the restaurant meeting Pochettino returned to Spurs training, and United appointed Jose Mourinho that summer.

But he was again touted as a contender when Mourinho was dismissed two years later.

Levy sacked him in 2019, but Pochettino has remained respectful and has since praised Levy’s impact.

On the field, his time at Spurs became famous for relentless intensity - a high energy approach with a high press. Think flying full-backs Danny Rose and Kyle Walker, and all-action midfielder Mousa Dembele. For another example of him getting the best from players look at Cole Palmer. The Englishman has never since replicated his numbers from 2023-24, Pochettino’s only season at Stamford Bridge.

The football, admittedly, was a little chaotic at Chelsea. It lacked control, and not even a mountain of meat could prevent him from being quizzed about his style of play in press conferences.

Chelsea’s fans never took to Pochettino. He was the face of Spurs, after all. Pochettino might be walking his dog – a Rhodesian Ridgeback called Sansa, named after the Game of Thrones character Sansa Stark – and the odd supporter would happily stop him to have a cosy chat. But he was always going to struggle to win over the supporters in their entirety.

He once said the first time he felt a connection with Chelsea’s supporters was in April 2024 after defeating Erik Ten Hag’s United 4-3, when Cole Palmer scored twice in the 10th and 11th minutes of stoppage time. That was 11 months after he joined.

He never quite won over the fanbase of Chelsea, where his football was chaotic and his high-intensity values led to accusations that he had worsened their injury crisis

One source at the time said Pochettino wanted ‘machines’ who had the physicality to play the high-energy philosophy he craved, and like at Spurs there was indeed a great deal of high-intensity running involved in training.

However, this talk of his sessions being fitness-focused led to accusations that his methods were contributing to their crippling injury list. Pochettino disputed that, telling us he did not come ‘from Mars’ and that he knew how to manage footballers better than those on social media. He was extremely defensive whenever asked if his training was primitive.

It would be fair to debate whether United have the players to play this way – and indeed how Pochettino himself would respond to the changing landscape of the Premier League.

Spurs boss Roberto de Zerbi noted recently that upon his return to the Premier League after two years away that almost all clubs now play a more physical game than when he was at Brighton. With more teams doing the same thing that once made a Pochettino side different, how would he adapt?

We are told leaving his job in charge of the USA is more contractually feasible now than it was before the 2026 World Cup when Spurs made checks last season. He has a buyout clause, apparently worth around £7million, though that does not include the compensation required to recruit Perez and Co.

Should United require any character references, they need only ask their own Luke Shaw. He wrote the epilogue of Guillem Balague’s book Brave New World: Inside Pochettino’s Spurs, having played under him at Southampton. Shaw admitted in those pages that he would love to work with his old coach again someday. Pochettino was there for him when Shaw broke his leg – still a man-manager even when they were no longer working together.

But United could also ask any number of Chelsea players who worked with Pochettino. They largely loved him. At the time of his dismissal in May 2024, one agent of a senior figure in the changing room told us that their client was ‘devastated’. Nicolas Jackson, formerly of Chelsea and now of Aston Villa, responded to Pochettino leaving by posting two face-palm emojis on Instagram.

Pochettino had his favourites. Conor Gallagher was one of them, and the Cobham graduate's contractual situation seemed to be a source of angst between the coach and Chelsea’s hierarchy.

Players tend to love Pochettino - Luke Shaw (right), one of Manchester United's veterans, had a strong relationship with him at Southampton

His power waned at Chelsea - the club hired a set-piece coach after he insisted he didn't want one, and he was gone before the specialist arrived

As noted, Pochettino was a builder hired to work with one of the Premier League’s youngest squads but did not get the time he wanted to mould Chelsea and never hid his desire for more power at Stamford Bridge. It is said they once had the chance to sign Ansu Fati from Barcelona on favourable financial terms, but did not go through with the transfer after the manager decided he was not for him.

We did get a glimpse of Pochettino’s waning power when Daily Mail Sport asked him once whether he would like a set-piece specialist adding to his staff. He dismissed the idea, suggesting he did not need one. Chelsea then lost the Carabao Cup final when Liverpool’s Virgil van Dijk headed in the winner in extra time from a corner, with Gary Neville coining the ‘blue billion-pound bottlejobs’ jibe.

A month later, Chelsea agreed a deal to hire Bernardo Cueva from Brentford to lead a new department dedicated to set-pieces. Pochettino responded by reminding us: ‘I am the head coach and I am going to decide if some people are going to be with me or not, or if I’m going to add more people or not to the touchline.’ He was gone before Cueva arrived in the summer.

An end-of-season review was held in May 2024, and Pochettino, along with Chelsea’s hierarchy, decided after a single season that it was not working. Not for him, and not for them.

Now those at the helm of United need to determine whether he is the man to bring the good vibes back, perhaps also with a little meat.

#2153. Elezioni FIGC, Galliani: “Se vincessi le elezioni confermerei Mancini”

Adriano Galliani, candidato alla presidenza della FIGC, ha parlato a margine della presentazione della quarta edizione del trofeo Italo Galbiati all’Auditorium Banco Bpm: “Se vincessi le elezioni FIGC confermerei Mancini? Non sono presidente, ma certamente sì. Il calcio ha già abbastanza problemi, servono serenità e tranquillità. Io continuo a essere convinto che non si possa dare un voto due volte, perché così qualunque candidatura può essere tolta all’ultimo minuto e in qualunque può diventare una compravendita di voti. È un vulnus, va regolamentato. Cinquanta anni di calcio mi hanno imposto questa decisione, arriviamo sereni e tranquilli al 14 dicembre Ci sono due candidati, uno vincerà”.

#2152. Figc, Galliani risponde a Malagò

Resta la sua obiezione sulla possibilità di votare due volte; alle elezioni del 14 dicembre ci saranno due candidati.

Adriano Galliani rinuncia al ricorso contro l’ammissione della sua candidatura e guarda al voto del 14 dicembre. A margine del Premio Galbiati, ai microfoni di Sky Sport, ha risposto a Giovanni Malagò, commissario straordinario della FIGC, che aveva definito signorile la sua scelta.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Galliani resta convinto della propria posizione: «Continuo ad essere convinto che non si possa dare un voto due volto. Altrimenti qualunque candidatura può essere tolta all'ultimo minuto. All'improvviso ti viene fatta una revoca e cade la candidatura». Spiega così la decisione di fermarsi: «Detto questo faccio calcio da 50 anni, ho pensato che il nostro mondo ha bisogno di serenità, tranquillità, abbiamo tanti problemi, allora ho pensato di non fare niente e andiamo al 14 dicembre».

Alla domanda se la rinuncia renderà più serena la campagna elettorale, risponde: «Assolutamente sì, si vota fra 4 settimane, ci sono due candidati e vedremo cosa succede».

Galliani racconta come ha cambiato idea sulla candidatura: «Avevo detto di no in precedenza. Mi hanno convinto le telefonate, oltre che di qualche club, dell'AssoCalciatori e dell'AssoAllenatori». Aggiunge: «Ulivieri mi ha detto che gli anni migliori per i rapporti con la Lega Calcio, di cui sono stato rappresentante, sono stati secondo lui quando c'ero io. Calcagno mi ha detto il suo maestro, l'avvocato Campana, gli aveva detto a sua volta che i rapporti migliori c'erano quando io ero presidente di Lega. Così mi hanno convinto».

Sul programma dichiara: «È molto lungo, l'ho presentato e depositato. Ora sono qui per premiare la memoria del grande Baresi e vi rimando al programma, non vorrei parlare troppo tempo».

Interpellato sulla Nazionale, Galliani risponde: «Non voglio parlare di Nazionale per evidenti motivi, quindi parliamo di Galbiati». Ricorda di averlo trovato al Milan al suo arrivo nel 1986 e di averlo poi voluto a Monza: «Ha insegnato a calciare a Ibrahimovic. Ibra era fortissimo, ma non sapeva calciare in porta, Italo glielo ha insegnato. Poi da talent scout scovava giocatori, ce ne sono pochi bravi come lui».

#2151. AC Milan among European sides keen on 17-year-old QPR breakout star

QPR have made a fine start to this season and currently occupy the final Championship play-off spot. The likes of Pierce Charles and Ronnie Edwards have earned the plaudits, while Richard Kone is finding his form again in attack, but a real breakout star has been 17-year-old Leon Scarlett.

Scarlett joined the club from Arsenal in April 2025, and so far this season he has made six appearances and clubs are starting to notice. Anthony Joseph has said that European giant AC Milan are tracking his progress after his start to the campaign.

The Italian side are not the only side keen, with Eintracht Frankfurt also keeping tabs on Scarlett’s situation. Both sides have expressed interest in the winger, who is already a Wales under-21 international.

QPR youngster Scarlett is attracting growing attention after breaking into Julien Stephan’s first-team plans this season, with AC Milan and Eintracht Frankfurt reportedly monitoring his progress.

The 17-year-old Welsh winger joined QPR in January 2025 after spending around a decade in Arsenal’s academy, having been released by the Gunners. Primarily operating on the left, Scarlett is known for his explosive acceleration, close control and ability to beat defenders in one-on-one situations.

He made his senior debut aged 16 in May 2026 and has featured regularly from the bench, but he did start against Preston back in September, accumulating around 130 minutes. He has also represented Wales at multiple youth levels, including the U21s.

Manager Stephan has praised his personality and willingness to express himself in first-team training, with QPR viewing him as an exciting prospect. He is playing ahead of his age right now and QPR fans have every right to be excited about Scarlett’s potential.

QPR must resist interest from AC Milan and Eintracht Frankfurt in Scarlett, with the 17-year-old better off continuing his development at Loftus Road.

Scarlett has enjoyed a rapid rise since leaving Arsenal’s academy, earning first-team opportunities under Stephan. However, with only around 130 Championship minutes to his name and no senior goal or assist yet, a move abroad would arguably come too soon.

Stephan has a proven record of developing young talent, while QPR are placing increasing emphasis on their academy pathway. Scarlett’s explosive pace, close control and ability to beat defenders make him an exciting prospect, but he needs regular exposure to senior football to refine his game.

A move to Milan or Frankfurt could leave him fighting for opportunities in youth sides rather than progressing at first-team level. QPR should keep hold of Scarlett, gradually increase his involvement and allow his performances and value to grow before considering a big-money departure.

#2150. L'UEFA ha trovato l'antagonista di Infantino: Diallo candidato alla presidenza FIFA

Il calcio europeo ha scelto il proprio candidato per la presidenza della FIFA. Philippe Diallo, numero uno della Federazione calcistica francese, ha accettato l'invito delle associazioni nazionali rappresentate nel Comitato esecutivo UEFA e sarà lo sfidante di Gianni Infantino alle elezioni in programma nel marzo 2027. La candidatura arriva con il sostegno compatto dell'Europa e dovrà ora essere formalmente ratificata dalla FFF.

La decisione è arrivata al termine di una riunione presieduta dal presidente UEFA Aleksander Ceferin. In una nota ufficiale, l'organismo continentale ha comunicato: "Le associazioni nazionali rappresentate nel Comitato esecutivo UEFA hanno invitato Philippe Diallo, presidente della Federazione calcistica francese, a candidarsi alle elezioni per la presidenza della FIFA nel marzo 2027 e hanno unanimemente garantito il loro pieno sostegno".

La UEFA ha sottolineato anche il percorso del dirigente francese, che può vantare esperienza in diversi ambiti del calcio. Diallo ha guidato l'organizzazione dei club professionistici francesi, ha fatto parte del consiglio della Lega professionistica francese e degli organismi FIFA dedicati alla risoluzione delle controversie, prima di diventare presidente della FFF e membro del Comitato esecutivo UEFA nel 2023.

Nel comunicato, la UEFA ha evidenziato anche le motivazioni alla base della candidatura: "Alla luce delle serie preoccupazioni riguardo alla direzione intrapresa dalla FIFA, le federazioni nazionali hanno chiesto una rinnovata unità e fiducia all'interno della comunità calcistica mondiale". L'obiettivo indicato è quello di una FIFA più focalizzata sullo sviluppo del calcio in tutte le regioni, con elevati standard di governance e una maggiore collaborazione con federazioni, confederazioni e tutti gli attori del movimento.

Diallo ha accettato la candidatura, che sarà sottoposta alla ratifica del Comitato esecutivo della Federcalcio francese.

#2148. Sarina Wiegman receives honorary damehood from Prince William at Windsor Castle - after leading Lionesses to back-to-back European titles

Published: 14:28 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 19:04 EST, 10 November 2026

Lionesses manager Sarina Wiegman has been awarded an honorary damehood by Prince William after leading England to back-to-back Euro wins.

Wiegman is Dutch and therefore cannot be made a substantive dame. Instead she has picked up the honorary title, because the Netherlands does not have a British monarch as its head of state.

She said upon being awarded the title at Windsor Castle on Tuesday: 'I feel very privileged to receive my honorary Damehood from His Royal Highness The Prince of Wales at Windsor Castle today.

'It is a day I will never forget and made even more special by sharing it with members of my family.

'I would like to again express my sincere gratitude for this honour and for the continued respect and warmth I experience from the English people. I thank everyone for their support.'

Wiegman was named in the King's New Year's Honours list at the end of 2025, alongside several members of her England team.

England manager Sarina Wiegman (left) has been awarded an honourary damehood by Prince William (right)

Captain Leah Williamson was awarded a CBE, with Alex Greenwood, Keira Walsh, Georgia Stanway and Ella Toone all being honoured with MBEs.

Wiegman said at the time: 'I would like to express my sincere gratitude for this honour. When I first arrived in England, I could never have imagined the respect and warmth I've experienced from the English people. I deeply thank the fans for their support.

'This title is a reflection of my exceptional team of players and staff. My congratulations go to Alex, Ella, Georgia, Keira, Leah and also Dr Ritan Mehta who have all been celebrated for their brilliant contributions.

'Our team values mean that when there is recognition for one, then there is recognition for all. I want to pay tribute to every member of our EURO squad. They should all be celebrated for everything they brought to the pitch and for the positive role they play in society. I'm proud to work alongside such a dedicated group of people.'

The former Netherlands coach took over the England team in 2021 and led them to success at Euro 2022 and Euro 2025.

She also reached the World Cup final in 2023, and signed a new two-year contract extension earlier this year, which will take her to the end of the 2029 Euros.

#2147. Lazio, Canovi frena: "Ottimo avvio, ma deve ancora affrontare le squadre più forti“

Le parole diDario Canovi sull'avvio di stagione della Lazio, tra ottime prestazioni e sfide ancora da affrontare.

Dario Canovi, ha parlato ai microfoni di TuttoMercatoWeb.com. Nell'intervista ha commentato il momento della Nazionale e ha detto la sua sul campionato, definendo la Lazio la grande sorpresa del torneo.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il Belgio è una grande squadra. Le grandi squadre nascono da un grande centrocampo e il Belgio lo ha. In Nazionale si vede il vero De Bruyne, noi abbiamo contrapposto calciatori che non possono certo essere considerati di pari livello. L'Italia è una squadra che esprime i valori del calcio italiano, guardate anche sul piano fisico: il Belgio ci ha sovrastato. È un bravo allenatore. Ma il materiale a disposizione è questo. E quando incontriamo le squadre che giocano il calcio attuale si vedono i valori. Mi spaventa vedere quale sarà il programma del nuovo presidente federale. Con Malagò, tutta la vita. All'inizio non ero favorevole a lui, considerato anche che sono suo amico. Pensavo non avesse il coraggio di fare certe cose, invece ce l'ha. Dopo il caos ha scelto lui Mancini e non s'è fatto imporre altri nomi. E ha preso l'allenatore migliore, quello più giusto.

La Lazio è la sorpresa e sta offrendo prestazioni importanti, ma deve ancora affrontare le squadre più forti. Tranne la Juve che per me è l'altra grande delusione di questo campionato.

A Radio Napoli Centrale, nel corso di Sempre Con Te è intervenuto Antonio Giordano, giornalista de La Gazzetta dello Sport: “De Bruyne non lo lascerei mai, lo incatenerei a Castel Volturno. Può succedere che De Bruyne voglia andare via, per una scelta di vita, ma allora non hai altro da fare. Le parole della moglie, di tre mesi fa, le sappiamo già. Si è scatenato una bufera. Ma il tono cambia tutto. Ho letto che De Bruyne a Napoli gioca male, in Belgio gioca bene. Perché Hojlund in Nazionale gioca meglio? Dal punto di vista stilistico, i due gol contro il Portogallo sono belli. Eh, ma è colpa di Allegri (ride, ndr). #2145. League One clubs target Linfield winger amid impressive form

Rhys Brennan’s form has attracted the attention of League One sides, who face competition from teams in the SPL. At this moment in time, none of the admirers are named in O’Rourke’s report.

Brennan, 20, is predominantly a right winger. He is out on loan with Linfield away from Bohemian, where his contract runs out next December.

Bohemian currently sit 3nd in the Irish Premier Division, whilst Linfield top the Northern Irish Premiership, having won 12 of their 16 matches so far. Brennan has contributed well to their success, locking down a starting berth on the flanks.

Brennan broke into the Bohemian side in 2025 and has since developed on loan at Cobh Ramblers before Linfield. A move to England would constitute a major step in Rhys Brennan’s career, should it take place.

Brennan’s debut for Bohemian came against Waterford at the end of the 2024 campaign. He soon followed that up with a first goal the following Monday, scoring as his side overturned a 2-0 deficit to win 3-2 against Shamrock Rovers.

Brennan has also featured for the Republic of Ireland U19s. The winger made his debut for the side away against Slovakia in a 3-1 friendly victory back in March 2024.

The young winger is an energetic presence on the wing. He’s shown he can offer a dual threat of goals and assists, but his crossing ability and work rate is where he stands out.

Furthermore, Brennan is a confident presence on the wing. He leads the way for Linfield with regard to successful dribbles per match this season, averaging 1.9 successful dribbles per 90.

This season is the first season where Brennan is fully flourishing in senior football. Should he make the move to England, he might need time to adapt given the step up from Northern Ireland to League One.

Brennan will not walk into a side challenging for promotion and integrate himself with regular minutes. Equally, a relegation battle would not be beneficial for his career.

A move to a mid-table side seems safe, giving Brennan a low pressure environment to develop and express himself on the wings.

Linfield will be reluctant to lose Rhys Brennan this season. However, should the player’s form continue, he may be a serious candidate for a move in the January transfer window, and a decision will ultimately be made by his parent club Bohemian.

#2143. The 3 summer targets Derby County should pursue again in January, including Anis Mehmeti

Derby County have had a tough season, but entered the November international break on a high by beating Bolton Wanderers in their last game. It buys John Eustace more time in the dugout as DCFC look to turn around their fortunes.

The Rams will have eyes on the January transfer window as an opportunity to strengthen their depleted squad. Injuries have forced them into the free agents market, signing Shane Duffy and Jack Robinson, but more long-term fixes can be found by targeting some of the summer’s targets who slipped through the fingers.

Here, we put forward three summer targets who Derby County should have back on their radar.

Derby County were among the clubs interested in signing Mattie Pollock from Championship rivals Watford in the summer. However, he ended up staying at Vicarage Road and was named as club captain.

Pollock has publicly spoken about how earning the captaincy made him feel confident that he was in the right place. But, with the Hornets struggling and doubts surrounding their long-term direction amid the Pozzo family’s ownership, a new opportunity could be tempting this January.

The Rams may not be in a better place in the league, but there is more hope for long-term prosperity at Pride Park than there is at Vicarage Road. Paying to sign Pollock as the present and future of their backline would be a statement of intent and a big step in the right direction away from signing older players.

Another player on the Derby County radar was Anis Mehmeti, but like Pollock, he stayed put. The Albanian had wide Championship interest after Ipswich Town’s rise to the Premier League, but he decided to compete for his place at Portman Road.

That has not panned out as hoped, and the attacking midfielder should look to make a winter exit. Mehmeti is a proven star in the Championship, offering a dual threat of goals and assists as a No. 10 or on the left-wing.

He could be the creative spark Eustace’s Rams side needs. Entrusting him with an influential role in the team could help DCFC win the race for his signature, as other second tier sides are sure to be interested.

Last but not least is James Tavernier, who some supporters may view as an underwhelming addition. Granted, he is now 35, but the experience and leadership he would bring to an unstable right-back position would not go amiss.

Tavernier only joined Maccabi Tel Aviv in the summer, but his deal is up at the end of the season, and he could be tempted by the chance to end his career back in England. He would also bring impressive set-piece ability with him, offering Derby County a much-needed route to chances.

Tavernier might not be a long-term option but it would take the pressure off a struggling Max Johnston and decrease the reliance on the likes of Joe Ward and Ryan Nyambe.

#2142. Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 16 novembre

MILAN, OFFERTA DA 70 MILIONI PER ENDRICK MA MOURINHO DICE NO. INACIO SUBITO, VAN DIJK SOGNO A ZERO. NAPOLI, SPUNTA ZIRKZEE PER L'ATTACCO: LUCCA E LANG IN USCITA. ANGUISSA, IL MARSIGLIA TROVA L'ACCORDO MA MANNA CHIEDE 6 MILIONI. ROMA, OCCHI SU CAMBIAGHI MA IL BOLOGNA FA MURO. JUVENTUS, ASSALTO DALLA PREMIER PER MCKENNIE E ALAJBEGOVIC. COMO, PERRONE VERSO IL RINNOVO FINO AL 2031. ATALANTA, SCALVINI HA UNA CLAUSOLA DA 50 MILIONI. TORINO SU CAMARDA, IL VENEZIA CERCA BONAZZOLI

Il mercato di gennaio si avvicina e le società di Serie A iniziano a definire le strategie per rinforzare le rispettive rose. Il Milan prepara un investimento importante in difesa e sogna Endrick, il Napoli cerca alternative in attacco, mentre la Roma continua a monitorare esterni offensivi. Non mancano i movimenti in uscita e i rinnovi, con diversi club impegnati a blindare i propri giocatori più rappresentativi.

Partiamo dal Milan, che vuole regalare a Ruben Amorim almeno un nuovo difensore centrale. Secondo la Gazzetta dello Sport, il primo obiettivo è Gonçalo Inacio dello Sporting Lisbona, individuato come possibile rinforzo già a gennaio per completare il reparto insieme a Pavlovic. Per la prossima estate resta invece viva la suggestione Virgil van Dijk, in scadenza con il Liverpool e potenzialmente disponibile a parametro zero. In attacco il grande sogno è Endrick: i rossoneri avrebbero presentato al Real Madrid una proposta da 60 milioni, incrementabili fino a 70, con recompra a 80 milioni per gli spagnoli. José Mourinho, però, non vuole privarsi del brasiliano. Il Milan potrebbe sfruttare anche i rapporti tra RedBird e Roc Nation, agenzia che cura gli interessi del giocatore. Per il futuro piace inoltre Leon Scarlett, esterno offensivo classe 2009 del Queens Park Rangers seguito anche dall'Eintracht Francoforte.

Il Napoli prepara almeno due o tre acquisti per gennaio. In attacco resta viva la pista Varela, ma il Monza continua a opporsi alla cessione. Per questo Giovanni Manna valuta anche Joshua Zirkzee, poco utilizzato dal Manchester United e già cercato in estate da diverse società italiane. Gli azzurri riflettono contemporaneamente sulle possibili uscite di Lorenzo Lucca, per il quale si valuta un prestito, e Noa Lang, che potrebbe invece partire a titolo definitivo. Per le corsie offensive piacciono Alessio Cacciamani del Torino e Jamie Gittens del Chelsea, quest'ultimo potenzialmente disponibile in prestito. A centrocampo molto dipenderà dal futuro di De Bruyne e Anguissa.

Proprio Frank Zambo Anguissa è finito nel mirino del Marsiglia. Il club francese avrebbe già raggiunto un'intesa con l'entourage del camerunese, in scadenza con il Napoli nel 2027, ma vorrebbe acquistarlo a gennaio senza versare un indennizzo. Una soluzione che non convince gli azzurri, intenzionati a chiedere almeno 6 milioni di euro per liberarlo anticipatamente.

La Roma continua invece a cercare l'esterno offensivo richiesto da Gian Piero Gasperini. Tra i profili monitorati dal direttore sportivo Tony D'Amico figura anche Nicolò Cambiaghi, protagonista di buone prestazioni con il Bologna e recentemente valorizzato anche in Nazionale. Al momento non risultano contatti diretti e l'amministratore delegato rossoblù Claudio Fenucci ha già escluso una partenza a gennaio. I giallorossi osservano inoltre la situazione di Endrick, pur consapevoli delle difficoltà legate alla posizione del Real Madrid.

Il Bologna, intanto, valuta Lorenzo Lucca per rinforzare l'attacco. Raffaele Palladino apprezza il centravanti del Napoli, che potrebbe arrivare attraverso un prestito. Le difficoltà incontrate da Piccoli e Dovbyk hanno spinto il club a considerare nuove soluzioni per il reparto offensivo.

Il Torino ha chiesto informazioni per Francesco Camarda, destinato a lasciare il Milan in prestito durante il mercato invernale. Ignazio Abate conosce bene l'attaccante per averlo allenato nelle giovanili rossonere, ma la concorrenza è particolarmente numerosa, con l'Udinese e diversi club di Serie A e Serie B interessati. I granata si avvicinano inoltre al riscatto di Daniel Bragança dallo Sporting: l'obbligo scatterà alla quindicesima presenza di almeno 45 minuti, per ulteriori 2,5 milioni più un eventuale milione di bonus.

La Juventus deve guardarsi dagli interessamenti provenienti dalla Premier League. Il Manchester United di Mauricio Pochettino ha messo nel mirino Weston McKennie, mentre il Chelsea segue Kerim Alajbegovic. In entrambi i casi, però, la risposta bianconera è stata negativa: Luciano Spalletti considera i due giocatori fondamentali nella corsa alla qualificazione alla prossima Champions League.

Il Como lavora per blindare Maxi Perrone. Le trattative per il rinnovo del centrocampista argentino procedono positivamente e l'obiettivo è prolungare il contratto fino al 2031. Nonostante l'interesse manifestato in passato da Inter, Napoli e Juventus, il club lariano vuole continuare a costruire il proprio progetto attorno al giocatore.

Capitolo Atalanta, che ha ufficializzato il rinnovo di Lorenzo Bernasconi. Attenzione anche alla situazione di Giorgio Scalvini: il difensore ha recentemente prolungato fino al 2031, ma nel nuovo accordo è stata inserita una clausola risolutoria da 50 milioni di euro, potenzialmente decisiva per il mercato della prossima estate.

Il Venezia cerca un centravanti e ha individuato in Federico Bonazzoli uno dei principali obiettivi. Piacciono anche Artem Dovbyk e Casper Tengstedt, mentre per la fascia viene monitorato Samuele Angori del Pisa. Possibili diverse uscite, a partire da Albion Rrahmani e Toni Fernandez.

Chiusura sul mercato internazionale. Il Borussia Dortmund segue con attenzione Angelo Gobbo, talento del vivaio della Fiorentina autore di 12 gol nelle prime otto partite con l'Under 17. Il club tedesco valuta un investimento sul giovane attaccante, già legato ai viola da un contratto professionistico.

#2141. Scott McTominay reveals hospital dash and heart shock treatment days before Scotland's World Cup opener

By CALUM CROWE, SCOTTISH DEPUTY CHIEF SPORTS WRITER

Published: 12:25 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 03:20 EST, 11 November 2026

Scott McTominay has revealed he needed a procedure to reset the beat of his heart just two days before Scotland’s World Cup campaign kicked off back in June.

Heading into the opening game against Haiti in Boston, the explanation given from the Scottish Football Association was that McTominay had been carrying a sickness bug.

But, after the squad had flown from their training base in Charlotte, McTominay had actually been whisked away to hospital straight from the airport via a police escort.

The 29-year-old had shock treatment on his heart before then going on to play all three games as Steve Clarke’s side were knocked out at the group stage.

The Napoli midfielder subsequently underwent corrective surgery back at the start of the season and missed Scotland’s opening four fixtures in the Nations League.

Now back in the squad ahead of the upcoming double-header with North Macedonia and Switzerland, McTominay has opened up for the first time on his health issues.

Scott McTominay had a heart procedure two days before playing against Haiti

Scott McTominay in action during Scotland's 1-0 World Cup win over Haiti

Asked about what happened, he replied: ‘The sickness bug? You’re not going to say I have something to do with my heart two days before the first game of the World Cup. It’s not an ideal scenario being in the hospital two days before — well, technically 36 hours before the first game of the World Cup.

‘But you have to take the cards you’re dealt on the chin. It’s part of the game. There’s nothing I could do about it and the shock went well, I was back in normal rhythm then 24 hours later you’re playing a World Cup game.

‘The World Cup, it was difficult for me, to be honest. I had to go and get the shock on my heart. That was two days before the Haiti game.

‘You prepare so well, I was in such good condition and something like that happens. You’re thinking: “How has this happened to me?” But then you have to get on with it. That’s the cards you are dealt in football.

‘Sometimes things don’t go your way and you have to continue to do your best. It’s nowhere near an excuse but it’s something you have to look back on and say: “Well, it’s part of the process”.

McTominay was given full reassurance from the doctors and medical staff that the procedure had gone well, insisting it was never in his thoughts to pull out of the squad.

Asked whether it was playing on his mind across the three games against Haiti, Morocco and Brazil, he said: ‘No. I put it to bed. I tried my best to put it to bed anyway. Whenever you go under anaesthetic and you have to get put to sleep, it decreases the athletic performance a little bit and sleep and stuff like that.

‘You have to get back round to doing it again. It was far from ideal but those are the cards that you’re dealt, there’s nothing you can do about it.’

Some fans had questioned McTominay’s form throughout the World Cup, oblivious to the fact that he was effectively playing through an ongoing heart issue.

Asked whether the criticism bothered him at the time, he continued: ‘It’s tough. It’s never easy, things like that, but you can’t use things like that as an excuse because that’s just not in my nature.

‘I take whole responsibility for a lot of the things that we didn’t create or didn’t score the goals in the World Cup.

‘I can put my face out there and say that that was nowhere near good enough. But that’s part of football. You have to get away and think about it and reflect and come back a better player.

‘I didn’t know that’s what people were saying. I probably had an idea.

‘I’ve been criticised a million times before in my career before but I don’t let it get under my skin or worry too much about it. You know yourself as a professional footballer if you’re not quite where you should be.

‘For me, I had half an idea but you just try to block it out because if you think about negative things you might speak it into existence.’

#2140. Rotherham United fan favourite makes long-awaited international debut in big 3-2 win over Mexico

Rotherham United man Denzel Hall replaced Kyle Ming in a right wing-back role at the break and helped see out a vital win. The Reggae Boyz put themselves in pole position to reach the CONCACAF Nations League semi-finals ahead of Wednesday’s second leg, by winning the first tie 3-2.

It could have been an even more memorable night for the hosts as they were three to the good by the hour-mark. But, Mexico staged a late fightback to reduce the deficit, courtesy of goals from Gilberto Mora and Julian Quinones.

Hall isn’t the only Rotherham United player to represent Jamaica, with Brandon Cover also featuring for the Caribbean nation. However, he missed out on a call-up this time around.

This was Hall’s first call-up having been granted a Jamaican passport in October 2025 and looked set to play a key role in the final stages of their FIFA World Cup qualifying campaign last season.

Hall did feature for the Netherlands at youth level, representing them from U15 to U17, having been born in Rotterdam. But, the wealth of talent ahead of him blocked his pathway into the senior side.

With Jamaican heritage through his father, the Rotherham United man was eligible to switch international allegiance and did so last year to get a taste of international football on the biggest stage.

Despite his impressive performances in a struggling Millers side last season, the 25-year-old was overlooked for his first two opportunities to join the squad as the Reggae Boyz suffered a defeat to DR Congo in March to deny them a place in the World Cup.

Taking the less-travelled route from Rotterdam to Rotherham last summer, Hall signed from Heerenveen but was expected to leave ahead of the new season following their relegation.

Cardiff City and Preston North End were reportedly interested in the versatile defender but neither deal materialised. Since, he has started in all of Rotherham United’s League Two games this term.

A rotten run of form has left them in 22nd place with their last league victory coming against Northampton Town nearly two months ago, although progressing past Bromley in the FA Cup last time out could help inspire an upturn in form.

Hall’s performance against Mexico means he could earn his first start in Wednesday’s second-leg, with his athleticism and work rate in a wing-back role making him a valuable option to call upon.

Also in contention for Rotherham’s trip to high-flying Grimsby Town this weekend, Hall is set to play a key role in trying to help the Millers avoid relegation to non-league for the first time in their history.

#2139. Bradford City takeover: Everything we know right now

Bradford City are being linked to a potential takeover by a Thai-based consortium, so here's a round-up of the latest on whether this move might come off.

The international break couldn't have come at a better time for Bradford City.

Following an exceptional start to the season, the departure of manager Graham Alexander to Preston North End at the start of October hit the club hard, and they went into the break with just one win from their last six league matches and having slipped from the top of the League One table to eighth place, having replaced Alexander with the former Wycombe Wanderers manager Michael Duff.

But losing Alexander may not even be the biggest change to be coming to Bradford City this season. It's been rumoured that a Thai-based consortium are considering launching a bid to buy the club from current owner Stefan Rupp, with the group having been attracted by the club's recent upward trajectory and potential, given the size of attendances they were attracting, even while the team was misfiring in League Two.

This isn't the first time since Rupp purchased the club in 2016 that there has been an attempt to wrest Bradford City from his control, so here's a round-up of what's been said about ongoing reported interest in buying them, and what it could mean for a club who previously slipped from the Premier League to League Two in just six years, between 2001 and 2007.

A report from journalist Alan Nixon on Sunday morning has reopened conjecture that the Bradford City owner Stefan Rupp could be set to sell the club after ten years of ownership. Nixon confirmed on his Patreon that "Bradford City are a surprise target for a Thai-based group" who have been considering buying into the EFL.

Nixon confirmed that the prospective bidders have shown interest in buying a club before, and that, "Bradford could be for sale at the right price and the Thais, who have an intermediary with inside knowledge, are considering an official offer."

Do you think Bradford City would be better off under new ownership? Let us know in the comments below!

But before anything else, any new owners will have to get a takeover past Rupp, and that might not be completely straightforward. The German has been reported to be more open to selling since the club were promoted from League Two in 2025, with The Athletic's Matt Slater reporting in March 2026 that, "Rupp has been listening to offers for years but has showing a bit more leg ever since last season’s promotion."

Rupp has, however, said in the past that he won't sell up to just anybody. In 2021, he rejected an offer from the US cryptocurrency investors WAGMI United. They instead turned their attention to Crawley Town but, since completing their takeover of the Sussex-based club in April 2022, Crawley's position has waned considerably, with the Red Devils currently propping up the rest of the EFL and six points from safety.

Rumours that Bradford City could be sold have provoked a strong reaction from fans of the club, so here's a selection of what they've had to say.

This poll of fans showed a majority of those who voted being against any takeover of their club:

While this fan doesn't believe that these rumours should be a surprise to anybody:

This fan is concerned over what's happened at several other clubs who've come under far eastern ownership:

And this fan believes that Stefan Rupp has already demonstrated that he is the owner that Bradford City need:

Their recent slump in League One notwithstanding, the direction of travel that Bradford City have undertaken over the last couple of seasons makes them a prime target for those seeking to buy into the English game.

For years, it felt as though the Bantams had everything bar the team. They had a stadium which is fit for Premier League football, and attendances were extremely healthy, even when the team wasn't performing especially well in League Two. On top of this, being the only professional football club in a city of half a million people gives the opportunity for further growth, should they continue to rise through the divisions.

Under Alexander, the team was finally successful, winning promotion back to League One in 2025 following an absence of six years, and their first season back hinted that the Bantams weren't done either, with the team finishing in a highly creditable fourth place before losing 2-0 on aggregate to Bolton Wanderers in the semi-finals of the play-offs.

But there is one word in Alan Nixon's report which may cause Bradford fans to worry a little about the nature of the group who are now being linked with the club. Nixon described this consortium as "speculators", and that will raise concerns about the motives of this group among some.

There isn't anywhere near enough detail about this group to be able to draw too many inferences about their intentions for the club, though it's previously been reported that they are connected to the former owners of Oxford United. But "speculators" is a loaded word, and there may be concern over whether they will have their own interests at heart or those of the club should they take over.

And when speaking to the Bradford Telegraph & Argus at the end of June, Rupp confirmed that, "You get requests from time to time but they are tyre kickers, people without money, wannabe football club owners, or people you simply don't like," and that, "I'm the keeper of the keys and I would never hand it over to somebody where I have the impression he can't do any better."

The sale price, should it be the same as it was reported to be earlier in 2026, would be unlikely to be too much of a sticking point. The Athletic's report in March suggested that £10 million could be enough to persuade him to sell, with Bradford owning neither their own ground nor their training ground, but such a sum could still only be considered a drop in the ocean, were the club to ascend to the Championship or beyond.

Still in the FA Cup, and with an EFL Cup quarter-final at Manchester City to look forward to in the middle of December, Bradford City remain in a better condition on the pitch than they have been for years, and the big irony of their current position is that this very success may push up their sale price. Stefan Rupp has said before that he will only sell the club to the right buyer, but the true test of his principles may come when the rumours cease and concrete offers start to land on his desk.

#2137. Bristol City and Bristol Rovers fans both face the same issue right now

The two Bristol clubs don't meet often, yet their rivalry is one of the fiercest in the English game. Right now, though, City and Rovers fans are united in their dislike of their current managers.

We're right in the midst of sacking season in the EFL, and both Michael Skubala and Steve Evans are edging closer to the trapdoor, having failed thus far, with both sides having lofted expectations ahead of the campaign.

Bristol City have had those expectations since their run to the play-offs under Liam Manning in 2024/25, but a transfer window which saw plenty of investment into the playing squad, with the likes of Dom Ballard, Lorent Tolaj, Lisav Eissat and others arriving, has only stoked that anticipation that they could make a run towards the top eight.

Meanwhile, all the good feeling that Steve Evans created in the latter stages of last season and in the first few weeks of this year has dissipated, with the Gas routinely beaten and looking over their shoulders, which wasn't the plan at all this year.

While Bristol City fans would like to have maintained their play-off level in the last couple of years, there was some potential leeway regarding some form of a project over the summer.

The Robins had a little shake-up in the board over the past year, with Charlie Boss appointed CEO in February, a few months after lifelong fan Richard Scudamore became a member of the board and Sporting Director James Ellis arrived this past summer. This is their first full season at the club.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Bringing in Michael Skubala and investing in some promising players from League One and abroad indicated that this would be a multi-year project to try and get into the Premier League, but as it stands, Bristol City are sleepwalking towards the third tier, winning just two of their last 10 games.

More importantly, though, Skubala's brand of football isn't giving fans the belief that things will get better. They've scored just four goals at Ashton Gate, and lost their last home game 5-0 against Norwich City. To many, that was the final straw.

Evidently, Robins fans aren't having Skubala in charge anymore, and once you lose the supporters, it's hard to see a route back.

While Michael Skubala's situation is very much a project yet to get going, the fall-off at Bristol Rovers for Steve Evans these last couple of months has been head-scratching and rather alarming.

It was just six months ago that Evans was celebrating amongst the fans in their 4-0 win over Cheltenham Town, marking their seventh successive victory at the Memorial Ground and their eighth straight in league action. If he went into the crowd now, they'd probably have a different reaction.

The Gas were many's pre-season favourites in League Two this year after they ended last season with 11 wins in their final 14 games, and they started this term with four wins from five. Since then, it's been one win and eight defeats in 10.

The credit that Evans built up through the latter stages of last season has left the bank, and Gas fans are calling for a change.

Saturday's home defeat against Chesterfield leaves Bristol Rovers sitting 18th in League Two, with the main concerns coming from the effort of the players during this dry spell of form.

Bristol City made their decision over the international break. With fans calling for Michael Skubala's head, CEO Charlie Boss sent a message of confidence towards their head coach.

Regardless of what Robins fans think, there can be some admiration that the hierarchy hasn't panicked immediately and is willing to give Skubala time to try and turn the ship around and oversee the multi-year project he signed up for in the summer.

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

The schedule doesn't get any easier for Bristol City after the break, though, with Middlesbrough, Wrexham, West Brom and Swansea City coming up in their next four games.

City fans are going to have to stick with Skubala through this difficult period and try to will their side on to collect some points. If not, the Robins could be in a relegation battle as we head towards Christmas, and a sacking then would only be seen as a decision made weeks too late at Ashton Gate.

While there hasn't been a direct vote of confidence at board level, Steve Evans may still have a small amount of credit left to try and turn this dreadful form around at the Memorial Stadium.

Evans' predecessor, Darrell Clarke, was sacked after losing 10 straight league games, so the higher-ups have shown patience where it may have been running thin in the stands previously.

Do you think both Michael Skubala and Steve Evans will be in their respective jobs by 2027? Let us know in the comments.

There's also the possibility that things don't get better without Evans in the dugout, who has achieved success nearly everywhere he's been, especially in League Two, with him getting Rotherham United and Stevenage promoted from this level in the past.

Right now, both sets of fans in Bristol want their current head coaches to leave, but it doesn't seem like that will happen anytime soon. Only time will tell whether those decisions are the correct ones.

#2136. Cardiff City & Swansea City face joint Grimsby Town transfer cringe right now

Cardiff City, Swansea City, Grimsby Town, and Iwan Morgan are all enjoying rather different fortunes in the 2026/27 campaign, but there is an interesting connection between the three clubs.

The two South Wales rivals are back competing at the same level again and both have plenty of reasons to be optimistic about their respective seasons. Cardiff City are rebuilding impressively following their relegation, while Swansea City are beginning to look a far more competitive outfit.

Neither has yet had the chance to get one over their rivals in the league, but that will change at the end of November when they finally meet for their first Championship clash since 2024/25. There is, however, another story they might both be keeping an eye on.

Iwan Morgan looks like a serious star in the making at Grimsby Town, with the 20-year-old already establishing himself as one of the most exciting young attacking talents in the EFL for the League Two outfit. His latest goal involvements came via a brace against Swindon Town during a 4-1 away win on Saturday.

Having left Brentford over the summer, the forward has hit the ground running, registering five goals and five assists already this season. What makes his emergence particularly interesting is his previous involvement in the academies of both Cardiff and Swansea.

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

He left Swansea after three years there in 2023, joining Brentford. Prior to that, the Cardiff-born forward had been with the Bluebirds for nine years. Also, these are two clubs with excellent youth-development structures and a strong reputation for giving homegrown players opportunities to establish themselves in senior football.

Morgan could quite conceivably have been starring for either side by now, or at least featuring in some capacity, but instead he is making a name for himself at Grimsby. With his performances attracting attention, both Welsh rivals could soon be left wondering how such an exciting talent slipped through their fingers.

One of the biggest frustrations for clubs developing young players is that talent can sometimes be overlooked until somebody further down the football pyramid recognises its potential.

Youngsters can be loaned out to levels that do not necessarily reflect their ability, while others are allowed to leave without ever receiving a proper opportunity in senior football.

Morgan appears to be another example of a player who might have been ready for a greater challenge much sooner. His performances suggest he is already capable of operating at League One level at the very least, despite being only 20 and playing for a Grimsby side in League Two.

Cardiff and Swansea may have missed their opportunity, but Grimsby's gain is enormous. They have secured a player whose ceiling could be considerably higher than the level at which they currently compete. If he helps to fire them up, he could yet be a major asset in the third tier next season.

It is not as though either Cardiff or Swansea have been particularly poor in their recent recruitment strategies. Cardiff have rebuilt impressively following relegation, adding plenty of players under the age of 25 to an existing core of academy graduates who have already demonstrated considerable quality.

They now boast one of the youngest squads in the Championship, with a project that has genuine potential to develop further. Swansea, meanwhile, have turned to Jamestown Analytics to help shape their recruitment under Vitor Matos.

What do you make of Cardiff and Swansea's respective strategies of late? Comment below!

They were already becoming a more competitive and organised outfit under his management, but their recruitment has added further quality, with several signings arriving at peak or pre-peak stages of their careers. There is now a strong starting XI and enough depth on the bench to make a genuine play-off push realistic.

Neither club is getting everything wrong, which makes Morgan's emergence all the more frustrating. Swansea could have pushed to retain him and loan him out to a side like Grimsby. Instead, they have to watch on as he tears up the fourth tier. Both Welsh outfits will be keeping an eye on his development, though.

#2134. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2133. EFL clubs face uncertain future as Andy Burnham warns over major TV rule change

Prime Minister Andy Burnham has urged caution over scrapping the 3pm blackout, and EFL clubs may have good cause to back him over this.

With rates of the illegal streaming of matches continuing to rise, there has been much conjecture in recent times over whether it is time for the ending of the 3pm blackout for televised matches.

The blackout, which prevents the broadcasting of live matches from the Premier League and the Championship between 2.45pm and 5.15pm on a Saturday, has come in for increasing criticism in recent years from fans of clubs who are unable to legally watch matches between these times while their counterparts have the ability to do so.

But now an interjection from Prime Minister Andy Burnham has cast fresh doubt over whether the blackout will be lifted in the near future, after he explained that such a decision needs to be handled carefully in order to protect smaller clubs.

The BBC have reported that Prime Minister Andy Burnham saying that he is sceptical about the removal of the 3pm blackout. Burnham has said that he believes that any removal of the blackout should be handled extremely carefully in order to protect the revenues of smaller clubs.

Burnham has said that, "I would urge a bit of caution [on the blackout], because it does protect the income of lower-league clubs," adding that, "If you have effectively wall-to-wall Premier League football at any time, it takes away perhaps the incentive to go and watch the football league or non-league."

Do you think the 3pm blackout should remain, to protect smaller clubs? Let us know in the comments below!

Burnham, who is a long-standing supporter of Premier League club Everton, said that he wouldn't be taking a solely consumer-orientated view of removing the blackout: "I think it has to be considered carefully. I'm not going to take the pure consumer view here. I'll take the health-of-football approach to this particular issue."

And he also confirmed that his main aim with regard to this issue is the protection of English football's unique pyramid system: "I think it's about protecting the experience with the paying supporter, protecting the football pyramid. Those issues matter to me, first of all."

Andy Burnham is actively considering scrapping the law that bans football fans from drinking in the stands

The 3pm blackout was first introduced in the 1960s, at a time when the televised coverage of football was first really starting to proliferate and the attitude of clubs towards the broadcasting of matches was very different to today.

At a time when match-day revenue was how clubs made the majority of their incomes, there was deep suspicion on the part of owners and chairmen about what sort of effect the widespread televising of matches might have on revenue coming into clubs.

An attempt to show live Football League matches had failed in 1960 after just one match was shown, but with England hosting a World Cup in 1966 there was increased interest from broadcasters in showing matches. The Burnley chairman Bob Lord spearheaded the opposition, which resulted in the blackout being introduced. Live televised football from the Football League didn't return until October 1983.

Critics of the blackout argue that it is a relic from a bygone age. In an era during which matches can be illegally streamed from elsewhere in the world, they argue that allowing all matches to be shown on the television regardless of kick-off times would be beneficial to clubs, who could charge more for their rights, and for fans of big clubs who want to see all of their teams' matches live.

The counter-argument to this is that, while no-one knows for sure what would happen were the blackout to be lifted, it's difficult to imagine a scenario in which it would be good for smaller clubs. Anecdotal evidence has long suggested that smaller clubs take a hit when they have matches scheduled for the same time as bigger matches.

And it remains the case that smaller EFL clubs and non-league clubs remain more dependent on match-day revenues than bigger clubs. This isn't to say that it doesn't matter to Premier League clubs, for whom it remains their third-largest source of income. But without television deals of the value of the Premier League's or vast commercial contracts, the lower you travel down the football pyramid, the more dependent clubs are on supporters turning out for matches. A YouGov poll in 2023 found that 14% of match-going fans would be more likely to stay at home in the event that matches were shown on the television at 3pm on a Saturday.

Furthermore, supporters of retaining the ban argue that removing the blackout would be akin to letting a genie out of a bottle. The likelihood of removing the ban would be a one-way street, and even if the effects of it were more severe for smaller clubs than anticipated, the likelihood of it being reinstated would be extremely thin. And with the rights to Premier League or Championship matches still being extremely valuable, big increases to the amounts being paid for matches at that level would only further widen the financial gap between the wealthiest clubs and the rest.

The Premier League and EFL could, if they wanted to, remove all of their matches from the period covered by the 3pm blackout and ensure that all matches could be shown live on the television. But with matches already routinely being shown at lunchtime, early in the evening, and increasingly on Saturday nights, it seems unlikely that the bigger clubs would do anything but fill in the gaps with more live televised football in the event that it was removed.

Andy Burnham's comments will cause a mixed reaction. The 2023 YouGov poll found that 25% of all fans supported the blackout while 52% were against it, though these numbers changed to 44% in favour and 45% against when it came to match-going fans. Balancing these opposing interests could prove tricky for the Prime Minister, given the conflicting nature of the desire of bigger clubs to maximise their revenues and smaller clubs' fears over what effect loosening or abolishing it could have upon them.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2131. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2129. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.
