Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2128. IAN HERBERT: Classless Chuckle Brothers Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada are not fit to lace Michael Carrick's boots - they have treated a dignified Man United legend shamefully with this painful charade

Published: 07:34 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 03:39 EST, 11 November 2026

Both Michael Carrick and the man who has decided to sack him were present on Saturday night at a 40th anniversary dinner for Sir Alex Ferguson, staged in the dining suite where the Old Trafford dressing rooms used to be. It was lost on no one that Carrick, oozing class and humour that evening, spoke far better than Omar Berrada, Manchester United’s chief executive.

There were 100 or so present and Carrick had just been through the wringer of the 3-2 win over Roma but he did not let his predicament obscure the bigger picture. Carrick ‘spoke brilliantly’, actor James Nesbitt, who was also present, related. ‘The Roma result had just happened, but he showed a real gratitude to Sir Alex. He spoke with confidence and humour, but also with a real understanding of the enormity of what he achieved.’

Those who really understand United would not have been surprised. In the days when it was a classy, brilliantly run club, Carrick was the one who, perhaps more than any other senior player, would always look out for the rank-and-file staff – ensuring that the backroom teams were taken out for a drink at the end of the season, and that taxis home were laid on.

It was he who latched on to what was good about the Manchester United ‘family,’ ensuring that everyone from the reception staff to the laundry team were made to feel a part of it. Sir Jim Ratcliffe’s Ineos have clinically dismantled the old sense of a United ‘community' but Carrick’s return created a gratifying sense around Old Trafford, back in January, that it was perhaps not entirely dead.

All of which contributes to the unalloyed rage that many who love the club will be feeling today about the shameful and classless treatment of Carrick. A man forced through the ritual of driving into the training ground at 7.15am this morning by Berrada and United director of football Jason Wilcox – men not fit to lace his boots – when the whole world knows he is toast.

As Carrick, a professional to his core, went through this painful charade, Berrada and Wilcox – the Statler and Waldorf of the modern Manchester United – had had their secretaries arrange air travel to Monaco today for a meeting with Ratcliffe, which could not be brought forward.

Michael Carrick oozes class and humility but has been dragged through a painful charade by his superiors

Omar Berrada (left) and Jason Wilcox (right) are the the Statler and Waldorf of the modern Manchester United

There have been moments amid the recent tempest which point to United requiring a more commanding manager for the depths that Ratcliffe and his dismal men have reduced the club to. I could see from my vantage point close to the United dugout at their trip to Chelsea, two weeks back, that Carrick simply didn’t have the words to steer the ship away from that afternoon’s disaster.

But in the faces of men like Kobbie Mainoo and, before injury, Marcus Rashford, who have been restored to life and self-belief as United players, we have also witnessed Carrick’s faculty to see and know quality in players and give it full force.

A year ago, we were witnessing Ruben Amorim, the manager hired at vast expense by Ratcliffe’s football geniuses, run down Mainoo’s United career. In the insane mayhem of Sunday afternoon's 4-4 draw against Aston Villa, he was the team’s creative anchor.

The people who have actually mattered at United – the fanbase – appreciate the respect Carrick warrants. It’s why the anthem carrying his name was still coming down from the Stretford End and the old North Stand on Sunday.

And so, of course, does Sir Alex, even though you half suspect he wondered all along if Carrick had quite the bite for the toughest job in world football, while hoping against hope he’d thrive.

‘There is a casualness about him that causes people to misunderstand his value and his constitution,’ Ferguson wrote of Carrick in his autobiography. ‘His handicap was, I feel, that he lacked the bravado of Frank Lampard and Steven Gerrard. But his character has always been outstanding. He's a football man.’ Sir Alex considered Carrick a better player than both those two.

And now what you suspect will be Carrick’s valedictory days at the club he has served so well are presided over by executives whose own fitness for purpose at Old Trafford was an open question from the start.

Very few people around Manchester City viewed the loss of Berrada, their ‘chief football officer’, as something to be fearful about, when Ratcliffe decided to make him United’s chief executive in 2024. Some former City insiders privately wondered how on earth he’d landed a job of that kind.

‘There is a casualness about him that causes people to misunderstand his value and his constitution,’ Sir Alex Ferguson wrote of Carrick in his autobiography

Sir Alex considered Carrick a better player than both Frank Lampard and Steven Gerrard

United want to tell Carrick ‘face to face’ what the whole world knows. These people won’t even discern what gross disrespect they are affording a man who deserves far better

And if Ratcliffe thought he was accessing City’s intellectual property when recruiting Jason Wilcox as technical director, then that was dodgy logic, too. Wilcox had only run City’s academy – a grossly incomparable challenge. His comparable experience to the United role was a mere 15 months as Southampton’s director of football. They were in the Championship for some of that time.

These were the men jetting out to Ratcliffe’s tax haven to tell him who should and should not manage United. Thus subjecting Carrick to another phantom training session at the Carrington – two weeks after watching the Under 21s play there while those Chuckle Brothers put their heads together about sacking him, across the complex.

United are putting it around, would you believe, that they want to be seen to be ‘doing the right thing’ and ‘observing the right process’. Well frankly, many inside football view this leadership group’s decision making as an embarrassment.

Nesbitt expressed sympathy for Carrick. ‘I feel for him,’ he said. ‘I'm no pundit but I am a fan. Clearly there's dissatisfaction with the fans about what we perceive is going on, but I think he's trying his best.’

United want to tell Carrick ‘face to face’ what the whole world knows. These people won’t even discern what gross disrespect they are affording a man who deserves far better.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Former Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann is among the names linked with a possible move to Old Trafford if United

United have not announced any change in their managerial plans, so the report remains unconfirmed. Carrick is still in charge, but his results at the start of the new season have given the club plenty to think about.

Michael Carrick did enough during his interim spell last season to earn the job permanently. After taking over following Ruben Amorim’s departure in January, he won 11 of his 16 matches and guided United to third place in the Premier League, securing Champions League qualification.

Julian Nagelsmann has previously been linked with the Old Trafford job, and the latest report has brought his name back into the conversation. The 39-year-old’s representatives are reportedly open to hearing United’s plans, but there is no confirmed agreement or evidence that formal negotiations have taken place.

Carrick’s achievements last season bought him the permanent role, but the opening weeks of this campaign have left little room for further slip-ups.

#2126. Wolves and Southampton will wince at new £70m transfer revelation

Middlesbrough, Wolverhampton Wanderers and Southampton were just three of the clubs earmarked as potential promotion candidates from this season's Championship, and their early-season form proves exactly why that was the case.

Only five points separates all three clubs after 15 games, with Middlesbrough only second on goal difference, whilst Wolves sit two places and just one point behind Kim Hellberg's men and Saints find themselves seventh on 24 points.

However, Tonda Eckert's side would be positioned in front of Cesar Peixoto's men were it not for the well-documented four-point deduction imposed on the club relating to Spygate.

All three head coaches have plenty of difference-makers at their disposal, particularly in attacking areas, with 85 goals scored between their sides so far this campaign.

Wolves and Saints do boast key personnel in this area of the pitch, namely Fer Lopez and Cyle Larin, although both have suffered injury misfortune in recent weeks, but it is fair to say that, after a fresh revelation at the Riverside Stadium, both Peixoto and Eckert will be left feeling extremely frustrated.

Even though Saints confirmed the permanent signing of Larin from RCD Mallorca and Wolves sealed an emotional Molineux return for the currently injured Raul Jimenez in the summer, links between the two clubs and various hotshots didn't stop there.

Having excelled on loan at Oxford United last season, there were strong debates over where Will Lankshear's future lay, especially given Tottenham Hotspur's need for further firepower under Roberto De Zerbi.

FLW names its Team of the Season so far in the Championship

Off the back of an 11-goal campaign at the Kassam Stadium, Wolves and Southampton became two of many Championship sides linked with a move for the England youth international's signature.

Given Wolves only scored 27 times in last season's Premier League, it was no real surprise when a player with such strong current ability and future potential was identified as a transfer target, but they, like the South Coast side saw offers rejected, albeit figures were never revealed.

Middlesbrough have made great strides of progress since Hellberg's appointment 12 months ago, but one major criticism in the final weeks of last season was the Swede's team's inability to finish off games in which they were the dominant force.

Unsurprisingly, such a trend has continued into this season's early weeks, but after sealing an initial £10m deal with Spurs, which could still rise, Lankshear has certainly solved such a conundrum for the Teesside outfit.

Despite missing Boro's final two games before the international break, which, ironically, Hellberg's side fired blanks in, the 21-year-old's record of 13 goals in as many games matched the feats of ex-Fulham ace, Aleksander Mitrovic, from 2021/22, when the Serbian's 43 goals helped the Cottagers romp to the title under Marco Silva.

As well as his goalscoring exploits, Hellberg recently claimed how impressed he has been with Lankshear's all-round game at such a young age, which has already alerted clubs higher up the pyramid.

Middlesbrough are one of the Championship's best-run clubs at present, having made plenty of cash from the exploits of Djed Spence, Hayden Hackney and Morgan Rogers in recent times.

It seems a matter of when, rather than if Lankshear is the next cab off the rank in this regard, and with the January window looming, exit talk has already begun to surface.

Clubs such as Leeds United and North East rivals, Newcastle United, have recently been credited with interest in the star forward, but in an attempt to retain their prized asset, it has been claimed that Boro have set a remarkable £70m valuation on Lankshear's services.

Middlesbrough fans, what do you make of the valuation placed on Will Lankshear? Comment below!

In July, a five-year deal was agreed between the club and player, and whilst the figure may be eye-watering, it comes as no surprise that Middlesbrough are in no rush to sanction an exit, especially if Lankshear's goals can fire them back to the Premier League, in turn landing greater sums of cash in prize money.

As well as potentially landing his current employers a fortune in terms of transfer fees and the money which comes with promotion, Lankshear is in line to make Tottenham plenty of cash too.

The North London side were unable to agree a 20% sell-on clause with Middlesbrough this summer, but could land at least another £10m in performance-related add-ons, which would be no surprise if the striker's form continues in such a manner.

#2124. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

#2123. Ipswich Town agreement with Chelsea must make Will Lankshear think twice

Will Lanskhear is in the midst of one of the best individual scoring seasons the Championship has ever seen, to the point where there are murmurs of him being tipped to make a move to an elite club as soon as January.

Middlesbrough are reportedly dismissive of those links, which see the 21-year-old admired by the likes of Arsenal and Manchester United, and over the weekend, the Sun stated that Boro had slapped a £70 million price tag on the exciting frontman's head.

Ultimately, that price tag is an indication that Lankshear isn't going anywhere anytime soon, but it won't be long before those links to the traditional 'big six' sides gain serious legs. The 21-year-old was being suggested for the England squad before injury struck just before the international break.

But he wouldn't be the first young English frontman to make the step to a top club early in his career if he did move on from Middlesbrough in the next 12 months, and he'll have to be wary of being caught up in the expectation that would surround such a move.

It's been a much-discussed question over the past couple of years or so, as Harry Kane entered his 30s, as to who the 'next Harry Kane' would be.

Luckily enough, the now all-time record appearance maker for the Three Lions hasn't slowed down; if anything, he's gotten better. But just last summer, that successor looked to be Liam Delap, after he was one of the better performers in Ipswich Town's sole Premier League campaign in 2024/25.

The frontman shone under Liam Rosenior at Hull City during the 2023/24 Championship season, and earned a Premier League move off the back of that with the newly-promoted Tractor Boys.

Ipswich went straight back down, owing mainly to poor defensive numbers. Going forward, Kieran McKenna's side only scored six fewer goals than Everton and eight fewer than Manchester United. Delap was the main source of those, netting 12.

He only netted one brace, too, so he scored in 11 of his 37 league games that year. For his first year at the level in a relegated side, the then-22-year-old was being tipped for big things, and elite Premier League clubs were interested in him.

After relegation, Delap moved from Ipswich to Chelsea for a £30 million fee.

The Blues fended off interest from Manchester United, Newcastle United and Everton for his signature, and many expected him to net much more than the 12 he did at Portman Road in a side playing in the UEFA Champions League.

Delap contributed to Chelsea winning the Club World Cup in America during the summer of 2025, appearing in every game he was available for and scoring against Esperance.

He was sidelined just three games into the new season with a hamstring injury, though, and upon his return a couple of months later against Wolves in the EFL Cup, he was sent off after receiving two yellow cards in the space of seven minutes.

Do you think Liam Delap can still make it to the very top of the English game? Let us know in the comments.

Then-Chelsea boss Enzo Maresca called the sending-off "very stupid" and "embarrassing," and he couldn't really recover after that, even despite his former manager Liam Rosenior coming in after the Italian left in January.

Delap ended the season with one Premier League goal and two in 41 appearances in all competitions. His time at Stamford Bridge was quite a disaster, and he left this past summer. Somehow, Chelsea managed to bank £50 million for his services from Nottingham Forest.

Lankshear already has five more goals in around 1,200 fewer minutes this season than Delap did in his last Championship campaign in 2023/24.

It's evident that he's further along in his development at this stage than his compatriot, but the 21-year-old could look at Delap's trajectory in the past couple of years as an example of what not to do.

Collect every point to win the round. Don’t let Neil Warnock catch you.

Arrow keys or WASD to move. P to pause, M to mute.

Swipe on the pitch or use the pad to move.

Joining these big clubs is always a risk, as the amount of pressure on these strikers is massive. It's not just young strikers who fail, either. Look at Viktor Gyokeres, for example; a goalscoring machine at Sporting is now playing back up at Arsenal.

The weight of expectation is even bigger when you're an English striker, too, especially one who has been talked about for a call-up while in the Championship. There'll be a demand for Lankshear to hit the ground running, and if he didn't, he'd be the one being shot at online and amongst pundits.

Honing his craft and not running before he can walk would be wise for Lankshear. It seems as if Middlesbrough are set on keeping him past January, and the likelihood would be that, if they were promoted, he'd do at least one Premier League season at the Riverside, too.

That's not to say that Lankshear should remain out of the spotlight for the next five or so years, however. It's evident that the 21-year-old knows how to handle the mantle of being a line-leader for a side expected to win games at Middlesbrough.

His purpose in Kim Hellberg's side is to get on the end of the many chances that those behind him create, and he's not only gotten into the right positions consistently, but he's finished opportunities exceptionally.

FLW has debated which 15 fanbases generate the best atmospheres and more inside their home stadiums

Lankshear could probably make the jump to a Premier League side in the next year or so, but at Middlesbrough, he's in a position where he can afford to gain more and more confidence as the focal point of a good team, with there still plenty of time for him to make that step-up.

If he does go to an Arsenal or a Manchester United in the future, doing so as a 23-year-old with a hundred-odd appearances for Middlesbrough, some of which coming in the Premier League, would suit him much better in the long term.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Martinez had questioned whether Carrick was backed enough in the summer and suggested the club needed changes.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

Speaking about the situation on his YouTube channel, as quoted by Football 365, Romano explained why Martinez’s comments caused concern inside the club.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

Romano added that Martinez had spoken directly to those at the club after the incident.

“Licha apologised to Man Utd as a club. He spoke to the directors, he spoke to Jason Wilcox, and he spoke to Michael Carrick, who was also involved in this conversation.”

“So, the underlying message remains the same. Lisandro is apologising for the way he expressed himself after a game during a difficult moment for Man Utd, but there was a discussion about the substance of what he said.”

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

#2121. Will Lisandro Martinez play against Leeds after explosive rant?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Lisandro Martinez could return to Manchester United’s starting line-up against Leeds United this weekend, with the defender reportedly available for selection after the controversy surrounding his comments about the club’s summer transfer business.

TEAMtalk transfer insider Graeme Bailey has suggested Michael Carrick could bring the Argentine straight back into the team for the trip to Elland Road. The fixture is expected to be a hostile occasion, making Martinez’s potential involvement an interesting one.

“I am told that Martinez is available for selection this weekend, and there is every chance Michael Carrick starts him,” Bailey said.

The defender recently found himself in trouble after questioning whether Manchester United had done enough in the summer transfer window. He apologised to the club’s hierarchy and was fined, but the incident has not apparently created a divide between him and the manager.

Bailey also dismissed suggestions that Martinez’s comments were an attack on Michael Carrick, insisting the defender and his teammates continue to support the head coach.

“Martinez was not critical of Carrick – far from it. This isn’t a repeat of something like Mo Salah criticising Arne Slot last season. It is almost the opposite.”

According to Bailey, the United dressing room remains firmly behind Carrick despite the team’s disappointing start to the campaign. Senior players reportedly believe the current manager is still the right person to lead the team, rather than bringing in another face to replace him.

“Martinez and the squad are 100% behind Carrick. They know there is pressure after a poor start to the season, but they do not want to see a change.”

Bailey went on to suggest that the fallout may have strengthened the bond between the players and their manager.

“Martinez and the senior players firmly believe nobody can come in and do a better job than Carrick. If anything, this has brought the group closer together.”

#2120. Birmingham City told to avoid 'silly' Premier League transfer as £5m fee dubbed 'fair'

Birmingham City's season has got off to a flyer, and now plenty of discourse throughout the November international break has revolved around how Chris Davies could further strengthen his squad in the January transfer window.

Blues' recent form sees them top the Championship on goal difference ahead of Middlesbrough and Stoke City, and they return to action this weekend to take on the latter at the Bet365 Stadium.

Not many outside the walls of St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park envisaged such a remarkable start, but supporters in this part of the world are now dreaming of a first Premier League season since 2011.

As well as looking assured in defence, Davies' tactical reshuffles in recent months have made Birmingham a real threat in their attacking play, particularly on the counter-attack through the speed and direct nature of Liam Millar, Patrick Roberts, Jay Stansfield and August Priske.

Millar's predicament, in particular, has been a hot topic of debate in recent weeks given the nature of his loan move from Premier League newcomers, Hull City.

The Canadian international's impact in B9 has slightly decreased in recent weeks compared to his flying start, but Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore, has issued the sum of cash Blues should look to front up for his services.

Millar, 27, signed for Hull on a three-year contract from FC Basel in August 2024, making just 53 appearances at the MKM Stadium in the process due to a serious injury.

The 48-time international was a regular in Sergej Jakirovic's play-off winning team last season, but was loaned out to Birmingham in September despite scoring what could be a crucial winner for the Tigers against Coventry City on August 29th.

He has since registered four goals and three assists in 11 games for Blues, and when asked how much money the club should pay for a permanent transfer, Moore told FLW: "His current form, where he's contract-wise and the relationship he seems to have at Hull, I think £5m is fair for all parties involved.

"He started really well for us, but he's not banging in loads and loads of goals. I know that sounds harsh because he has had a very good start and he does look a threat. But I just think £5m is fair," he added.

"Hull get a decent value, his contract's running out. I know they have the extension on him, but I do think it's a fair price all-round, to be fair. It depends on how much more they want.

Birmingham City must thank Stoke City for beating Derby County in the race for Liam Millar's signature for one clear reason

"If they want £7m, I'd be looking at an add-ons deal potentially. But, I think £5-7m is more than fair. Anything else, you start to get a little bit silly.

"It all depends on where we are as well come January. If we're making a real push to get promoted, then do you want to put that money elsewhere because we've got Millar and then revisit in the summer?

"I think it depends on where we are and what the asking price is. But, right now, I think £5-7m is probably fair."

In September, Birmingham fended off competition from both Middlesbrough and Stoke for Millar's services, and it is a deal which has certainly paid off thus far.

Davies has two experienced options to select from on the left flank, with Demarai Gray also scoring three times this season after previous injury issues.

However, it is clear how much attention to detail went into making the deal possible, with the Blues boss revealing after the loan was confirmed: "I've tried to get him in a few transfer windows now - but never been able to.

"He's a player with a lot of experience of this league. We've had to be patient and finally we've got him. And the fact that he's coming to us says a lot, as he had a lot of options."

With Hull now in the Premier League, the market valuations of many players at the MKM Stadium have increased, and Millar was no exception to that rule, with it being claimed his current value sits at £4.1m.

That, of course, could increase again if his output at St. Andrew's continues to be strong between now and May in Birmingham's own promotion push.

Blues fans, how much money should Knighthead pay to sign Liam Millar permanently? Comment below!

Hull supporters were recently left perplexed by the Canadian's admissions in a post-match interview, with some even debating that he would never play for the club again.

However, the Tigers still have plenty to say when it comes to his future, as if they extend his contract until 2028, Blues would certainly be forced to cough up a greater sum of cash for his services.

“When you look at our forward options, we’ve got Morgan, who I just mentioned, who can operate as a second striker. You’ve got Heggebo as someone who can hold a ball up and bring others to play, then you’ve got Barney Stewart who operates as a target man or poacher.

“If Albion are to bring in another striker in January, it would probably be in a similar mould to Cameron Archer, who we tried to bring in, of course, but obviously won’t be coming here, someone similar to him who can stretch defences, who has speed and can add an extra dimension to Albion’s attack.”

When it comes to the addition of a striker this January, West Brom must eye up a more recognised and proven Championship goalscorer that can help cement them in the promotion conversation.

Albion haven’t seen a rich number of goals from their forward options so far this season, so it would seem puzzling for the club to add yet another unknown quantity into the mix who has no previous experience of playing in the Championship.

It would be surprising to see Albion make a move for Danns this January, with the Baggies needing to show much more ambition if they are serious about ending their six-year exodus from the Premier League.

#2114. Police 'are trawling through dodgy Fire Stick sellers' bank accounts to contact illegal viewers' of Premier League football and films

Published: 11:08 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 03:40 EST, 11 November 2026

A former detective has revealed the lengths authorities are going to in order to crack down on the use and sale of 'dodgy Fire Sticks'.

Law enforcement organisations across the UK are taking a tougher stance on these devices – and how they are used and distributed – with multiple people having been convicted and jailed in recent years for selling and supplying them.

The devices, which often cost anywhere between £30 and £100 – in comparison with around £30 a month for a Sky Sports subscription – give customers access to a range of premium TV services without paying, most notably in a football sense with access to live matches.

But Rav Wilding, a former detective who recently appeared on the BBC Morning Live show, has sent a fresh warning to individuals that use them.

Wilding said: 'People are buying them online usually on the black market and then they're getting all these services for free essentially.

'But it's a crime. It's not allowed. Owning the stick itself is not a crime, but using it for this purpose is.'

A former detective has revealed the lengths authorities are going to in order to crack down on the use and sale of 'dodgy Fire Sticks'

Wilding went on to say that along with a crackdown on sellers through house raids, authorities are now looking through their accounts to find out in further detail who is using the devices.

They then often contact those users in a new step, with it estimated that around four million people or households have got these Fire Sticks at the moment.

Wilding added: 'All of them are using them illegally. They're going for the distributors. They're going for the sellers. They are having a lot of success. But actually, once they've got the seller, they're going through their accounts.

'They're seeing who has been paying the money for these illegal things, and if they've been paying them for these sticks then people should possibly expect a conversation or two if the police want to know why they've been paying them money for this.'

Wilding revealed that this included checking bank statements to find out the information of individuals who have been paying for such a service, and authorities have been joined by subscription service providers in their clampdown attempts too.

'They (subscription service providers) are losing money to this, so they have been reaching out and are taking action against those people using them illegally as well,' he explained.

'So I wouldn't say it's something you should do. It is actually a crime and it can have a nasty fine, a huge fine and also a custodial sentence for users and sellers of these things.'

Rav Wilding appeared on BBC Morning Live and sent a fresh warning to users of the devices

Daily Mail Sport has previously reported FACT is playing a leading role in the crackdown on the devices, with the anti-piracy organisation liaising with police forces across the country.

And Kieron Sharp, chairman of FACT, added: 'We have a wide range of enforcement options - from issuing cease and desist notices to "knock and talk" visits - through to full criminal prosecutions where appropriate.

'We will take robust action against those involved - not only targeting sellers and distributors, but also those facilitating and profiting from the illegal activity.'

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.

#2110. Two days or two weeks – Michael Carrick future decision rolls into the international break

Manchester United will use the November international break to decide whether Michael Carrick stays on as head coach, after a three-game reprieve that delivered only one win.

According to The Guardian, Carrick came close to being sacked after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October.

Director of football Jason Wilcox and chief executive Omar Berrada discussed his position at the training ground the following day and agreed to give him three matches to turn things around before the break.

Those games brought a 2-0 defeat at Chelsea, a 3-2 win over Roma and a chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday. United conceded eight goals across the three fixtures, and defensive frailty remains the biggest worry.

Wilcox is now leading a review of Carrick’s position. He will consult Berrada before making a recommendation to Sir Jim Ratcliffe, who oversees the club’s sporting operations. Talks are expected to begin on Monday and continue through the break.

The league table makes grim reading. United sit in the Premier League relegation zone with just one league win in their last 10 matches.

If he does win a stay of execution, results will need to follow. Manchester United cannot be languishing in 17th place.

Carrick remains defiant. Speaking to Sky Sports News after the Villa draw, he admitted his side “haven’t won enough games” but insisted he expects to move forward.

“I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it’s got to win games, said Carrick after the late Villa draw.

“I think there’s more than enough [to turn things around].”

If United do make a change, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe is reported to be Wilcox’s preferred candidate. Howe has been out of work since leaving St James’ Park during pre-season, after four and a half years in charge.

In the meantime, there are no plans to strengthen Carrick’s coaching staff.

For now, Carrick is expected to be in charge for United’s next match, against Liverpool. Whether three games were enough to convince the hierarchy should become clear before then.

The trip to Anfield could be do or die for the decorated former United midfielder.

He’s not likely to survive a heavy defeat at the home of the club’s fierce rivals, but a galvanising win would surely buy him time and goodwill.

That’s if he makes it through the next fortnight before then. A decision or indication could be two days or two weeks away.

The only certainty at Old Trafford is uncertainty.

#2109. Monaco summit adds unnecessary drama to Michael Carrick situation

Michael Carrick arrived at Carrington for Manchester United training as normal on Tuesday morning, while his bosses were preparing to decide his future in talks on the Mediterranean.

Chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox are flying to Monaco to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe, with the BBC reporting that the pair will recommend Carrick is sacked.The Guardian has indicated representatives of the Glazer family will also be involved.

The club insists the Monaco summit was pre-planned and is not solely about the head coach and while that may be true, it hardly matters.By allowing the meeting to become public knowledge, United have turned a straightforward football decision into unnecessary drama, compounding an already difficult situation.

What does is say about Man United in 2026 that a manager is given three games to secure his job, then left to train the squad during an international break while the executives are flying off to rule on his future? It’s farcical.

The latest chapter is another muddled process.Wilcox and Berrada met at Carrington the day after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October and agreed to give Carrick three more matches.A defeat at Chelsea, a win over Roma and a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa later, they appear to have reached the conclusion many expected a fortnight ago.

Carrick deserves better than this.Whatever the outcome, a club that wants to be taken seriously should not be settling its manager’s future in the full glare of the media while he is still out on the training pitch.

Michael Carrick sacked by MUFC. Top lad, got the team in the Champions League against expectations. Gave it his all, night and day. Protected others, never threw anyone under a bus. Worked in difficult circumstances. Leaves with head held high. I wish him and his staff the very…

#2107. “Quite simply disgusting”: Gary Neville tears into Manchester United over Carrick sacking

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Gary Neville has launched a furious attack on Manchester United’s leadership over the way the club handled Michael Carrick’s dismissal.

United confirmed on Thursday that Carrick had been sacked, four days after the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa left them 18th in the Premier League with eight points from 10 games.

The club said it had reached the “extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary”.

Neville had already branded the process “classless” on The Overlap’s Stick to Football podcast before the announcement, pointing out that it was widely known Carrick was about to go while United were holding talks with potential replacements.

Speaking to Sky Sports after the decision was confirmed, the former United captain went even further.

“It’s just quite simply disgusting,” he said.

“That falls well below the standard of what you would expect in any other business, in any other industry. It would be constructive dismissal and you’d be getting sued.”

Neville took particular aim at the decision to hold the crucial meeting in Monaco, with chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox flying out to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe while Carrick was still taking training at Carrington.

“The optics of being in Monaco whilst the team, the staff, the people who are running the club on a day-to-day basis are in Manchester, it’s just not good and it reflects badly on the leadership,” he said.

It seems the only ones not to realise that are the trio above, but they aren’t stupid. How they explain it, or attempt to rationalise, it will be painful to listen to.

Neville also argued the club should have acted sooner. Looking back to the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth in October, he said United “effectively watched Michael Carrick resign on the pitch” as he waved goodbye to supporters at full time, only for the board to hand him three more games.

He summed up the episode as “indecision, lack of authority, just basically really poor leadership”.

It’s more than an episode, it’s an omnishambles that reflects the dysfunctional state of MUFC in its current guise.

United are now expected to move quickly for Mauricio Pochettino. The United States head coach is reported to have reached an agreement in principle with the club, which is prepared to pay his release clause.

First, they will need to wait until the USMNT completes its CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final double header against Haiti on Saturday and Wednesday.

Yes. Man United to wait until an international break to appoint an international manager…

For Neville, though, the bigger problem lies above the dugout and no manager can be the scapegoat for that massive issue.

#2105. BREAKING: Mauricio Pochettino agrees Manchester United deal to become new manager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have confirmed an agreement with Mauricio Pochettino to become their next head coach, with the Argentine set to take charge after the international break.

The 54-year-old will replace Michael Carrick, who was dismissed on Thursday following United’s disastrous start to the Premier League season. Pochettino is set to sign a two-and-a-half-year contract that will keep him at Old Trafford until the summer of 2029.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea boss is currently managing the United States national team but is expected to fly straight to Manchester after completing his USA international games.

Pochettino will first oversee the USA’s CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final ties against Haiti on Saturday and next Tuesday. He is expected to arrive in Manchester on Wednesday before taking charge of the club on Thursday.

Pochettino’s first taste of action as a United manager will be against Liverpool at Anfield on November 22.

Pochettino brings considerable Premier League experience after previous spells with Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea. He has also managed Paris Saint-Germain before the USA job.

His appointment makes him Manchester United’s eighth permanent manager since Sir Alex Ferguson retired in 2013.

Carrick’s departure came less than six months after he signed a two-year contract. His second spell in charge ended after United managed just one win in their opening 10 league matches, leaving them in the relegation zone.

Carrick’s coaching staff, including Jonny Evans, Jonathan Woodgate, Steve Holland, Travis Binnion and goalkeeping coach Craig Mawson, have all left alongside Carrick.

#2103. Man United Confidential: Jim Ratcliffe makes new 'brutal' cutbacks to ex-players' charity, Nathan Collins and Calvin Bassey among January targets, Luke Shaw eyes Anfield return and why club confiscated two anti-owner banners

By CHRIS WHEELER, NORTHERN SPORTS WRITER and NATHAN SALT - FOOTBALL REPORTER

Published: 12:15 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 03:42 EST, 11 November 2026

Sir Jim Ratcliffe is known for being ruthless – as Michael Carrick may be about to find out – and it appears as though the Association of Former Manchester United Players has once again paid the price.

Two years ago, Ratcliffe made front-page news at Christmas after it emerged he was cutting a yearly £40,000 donation to the AFMUP charity, which has been helping ex-United players since 1985.

Last week organisers were informed at the last minute that the second of its two annual dinners couldn’t be staged as usual in the Manchester Suite at Old Trafford and would have to move elsewhere.

So while plans were being made for Sir Alex Ferguson’s glittering 40th anniversary bash in the Heritage Suite on Friday night, the AFMUP had to rely on Hotel Football – part-owned by Gary Neville and the Class of ’92 – coming to the rescue to host their event over the road 24 hours earlier. Ferguson, it has to be said, has been a big supporter of the charity over the years.

There are now concerns that AFMUP will never be allowed back to Old Trafford with United understood to be insisting on a total of 400 guests, which is double what the charity can provide.

Previously, the AFMUP gave half of the £40,000 donation back to the club to hire the suite in April and September, raising additional funds through ticket sales and auctions.

Sir Jim Ratcliffe (left) is known for being ruthless and it appears as though the Association of Former Manchester United Players has once again paid the price

When Neville challenged Ratcliffe over the ‘cruel decision’ to cut the donation in a one-on-one interview in March last year and said the club should consider organising the dinner itself and asking current players to attend, the minority owner replied: ‘Well nobody suggested that, I would have supported that.’

As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada joined Ferguson and former CEO David Gill at the dinner the following month – when Neville, Andy Cole and Jonny Evans were also present – and donated a VIP package for the auction worth £20,000.

Director of football Jason Wilcox then appeared at the next event in September and gave a rare interview on stage. In April of this year, Bryan Robson and Steve Bruce formed a popular double act as the main attraction.

Thursday’s dinner, which had already been moved from September 24 due to a double booking, was a celebration of the life of Duncan Edwards, who would have been 90 on October 1. Former chairman Martin Edwards and Gill were both present along with former goalkeeper Alex Stepney.

The AFMUP’s official filmmaker and publicist John Gubba highlighted the latest setback on his Man Utd The Religion YouTube channel, saying: ‘While the red carpet is being rolled out for Fergie, the club have been brutally abandoning the former players’ association.

‘The latest cutbacks by Sir Jim Ratcliffe have resulted in another kick in the teeth for the AFMUP. Staging their big event at the Theatre of Dreams was always a status that made the former players and their guests feel part of the football club. Being turfed out at short notice leaves a bitter taste.’ You can watch the full video here.

United sources say the club helped to facilitate an alternative venue at Hotel Football due to unavailability in the stadium, and insist that the charity will be welcome back to Old Trafford in future.

Berrada was present at Ferguson’s celebration dinner on Friday in which the United chief executive spoke about the importance of building a culture at Old Trafford.

He was one of 100 invited guests for the event to mark 40 years since Ferguson was appointed as United manager, moving from Aberdeen to replace Ron Atkinson in November 1986.

A number of Ferguson’s old players were present and posed for pictures with him including Michael Carrick, Brian McClair, Edwin van der Sar, Robin van Persie, David May, Wes Brown, Paul Parker, Dion Dublin, Denis Irwin, Jesper Blomqvist, Ryan Giggs, Gary Pallister, Javier Hernandez, Clayton Blackmore, Jonny Evans, John O’Shea, Darren Fletcher, Ronny Johnsen, Lee Martin, Nemanja Vidic and Quinton Fortune.

Ferguson’s former No2 Mike Phelan was also there along with Gill and kit man Albert Morgan.

There were 100 invited guests for the event to mark 40 years since Sir Alex Ferguson (third right) was appointed as United manager in November 1986

Luke Shaw is targeting a return to action away at Liverpool a week on Sunday, Confidential understands.

The 31-year-old has not featured since October 10 when he played 90 minutes against Tottenham, missing seven games through to the current international break.

That absence badly exposed United's lack of depth at left back, an area the hierarchy failed to address in the transfer window despite a clear need. Diogo Dalot, who was targeted by fans when he came off against Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday, has played poorly while filling in on his unfavoured side.

With Patrick Dorgu now back in the mix, too, Shaw is hoping to be in contention for the trip to Anfield in what would be a timely boost for a defence that has conceded the joint-most goals in all competitions of any team in Europe’s top five leagues this season.

Nothing has undermined Carrick's time as United head coach more than the defensive problems that have seen his team concede 15 goals in their last five games.

United chose to delay the overhaul of their defence until next year when they focused on rebuilding the midfield last summer, but it has proved to be a costly misjudgment with Matthijs de Ligt only just returning to action after a year out and a spate of injuries to Shaw, Lisandro Martinez, Dorgu and Noussair Mazraoui.

It may force United into the transfer market in January and Confidential understands Brentford centre back Nathan Collins and Fulham’s Calvin Bassey have joined Jarrad Branthwaite of Everton on the list of potential targets.

United also want a striker after failing to offload Joshua Zirkzee and sign cover for Benjamin Sesko in the summer, but a loan deal is more likely.

Brentford's Nathan Collins and Fulham's Nigeria star Calvin Bassey have both joined United's list of centre-back targets

As if the prospect of relegation isn’t nightmarish enough for United after they dropped to 18th place in the table, the cost of going down would be astronomical.

Although parachute payments from the Premier League would cushion the fall in the unlikely event that United are relegated, kit sponsors adidas have the option to scrap their record £900million, 10-year deal which was announced in 2023 but only came into effect last year.

Under the terms of the contract, the German sportswear giant can cancel it if they give the club one year’s notice. Adidas can also halve their annual payment to £45m if United aren’t playing in the Premier League, and hit the club with a £10m annual penalty if they are not in the Champions League.

Two banners protesting against United’s owners were confiscated by stewards during Sunday’s 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford.

One that read ‘Jim Can’t Fix This’ was seized because its connection to Jimmy Savile was deemed to be inappropriate. Another declaring ‘We Don’t Want A New Stadium, We Want New Owners’ was taken down because it was blocking the view of some fans.

Club insiders say protests will always be allowed as long as they remain within the regulations. United kept a close eye on Sunday’s demonstration with a more visible police presence in the Stretford End and stewards asking fans as they entered that section of the stadium which group they were with.

The protest, which once again involved green and gold balloons being released before kick-off, was jointly organised by The Red Army, The 1958, Manchester United Youth Supporters Club, South Stand United and Retro Reds.

However, it seemed like a strange decision to confiscate the banners considering that United were in possession of them for more than 30 hours before kick-off since 10am on Saturday morning.

They are stored in the same place in the Stretford End before every game, so it was odd for United to demand that they be taken down when they could have refused for them to be handed out in the first place.

A banner protesting against United's owners was confiscated during the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa

While goalkeeper Senne Lammens was giving his worst performance in a United shirt against Villa on Sunday, the man he replaced at Old Trafford was faring a little better in Turkey.

Andre Onana kept a clean sheet in Trabzonspor’s win over Alanyaspor as they moved into second place in the Super Lig table behind Galatasaray.

It came three days after a Europa League defeat by Freiburg when Onana couldn’t resist a little light ribbing of English reporters at the game.

Asked by journalists from Daily Mail Sport and the BBC for a quick word, the Cameroon international didn’t disappoint. ‘I don’t speak English, bro,’ Onana said, in perfect English. Classic.

#2102. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.

#2101. Steven Gerrard explains his theory on why Alexis Mac Allister has been so resurgent this season

Steven Gerrard has offered a theory as to why Alexis Mac Allister has looked so resurgent this season compared to last.

The midfielder was one of the Reds’ most disappinting players of a dreadful 2025/26 campaign, especially having been such a key component in the preceding Premier League title triumph, but he’s been playing near to his best since Andoni Iraola took charge in the summer.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily (via Liverpool Echo), our former captain claimed that the 27-year-old subconsciously let his performance levels drop last season as he was privately consumed by trying to retain the World Cup with Argentina.

Gerrard said of Mac Allister: “I’ve loved him this season. I think last year, like a lot of the Argentinian players, they had a bit of a distraction with the World Cup coming up.

“It’s the way they are. The World Cup takes over everything in their lives, and I lived that with Emiliano Martínez when I was at Aston Villa. I just think it becomes a priority.

“I think it becomes a bit of a distraction. It becomes the most important thing in their lives, to be ready and not injured and make sure that the national team is the priority.

“I think with a lot of the Argentina players, I’m sure a lot of managers, if they watch this, will say: ‘Yeah, I’ve experienced that.’”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

It’s an interesting theory that Gerrard has put forward to explain why Mac Allister’s performances from last season to this have been so chalk and cheese.

Many footballers consider the World Cup as the pinnacle of their careers, and the Liverpool midfielder represents a nation where the sport is nothing short of an obsession.

Only the 27-year-old could truthfully answer whether or not he let his levels dip in 2025/26 because he was prioritising the global tournament in North America, but from a Reds perspective we’d hope that isn’t the case.

As a player employed by LFC, Mac Allister has a duty to give full commitment to representing the club. As disappointing as his performances were last season, though, we wouldn’t go as far as accusing him of not giving less than 100%, which’d be a serious indictment of an elite footballer.

Thankfully, the Argentine has looked much more like his usual self since August, to the point where Troy Deeney suggested that he would even inspire Liverpool to nudge their way into the Premier League title race if he maintains or improves further upon his current standards.

The 27-year-old has been vocal about his contract situation at Anfield, but if he keeps playing the way that he has been, securing a new deal for him should become a matter of priority for sporting director Richard Hughes.

#2100. Malo Gusto’s Kante Lesson Gives Chelsea A £75m Xabi Alonso Decision

Malo Gusto’s World Cup has suddenly become more than a France subplot for Chelsea. It is now a live assessment of whether Xabi Alonso inherits a right-back worth protecting, reshaping or trading at the peak of his market.

Chelsea’s own interview with Gusto this week underlined the human part of that equation. The 23-year-old spoke about learning from N’Golo Kante, calling the former Blue one of the best midfielders France have produced and stressing the value of his example on and off the pitch.

That matters because Kante is not just a nostalgic reference point. He remains the standard Chelsea supporters still use when judging humility, intensity and reliability in a high-pressure dressing room.

For Gusto, those qualities arrive at a decisive moment. Reports around the player’s future claim Chelsea value him at around £75million, with the imminent Marco Palestra deal creating a more crowded full-back picture.

That number looks aggressive, but the logic is clear. Gusto is young, capped by France, under contract, Premier League-tested and capable of playing in several defensive structures.

🚨🚨| NEW: Chelsea are open to selling Malo Gusto this summer, with his agent already in 𝐓𝐀𝐋𝐊𝐒 with Manchester City and Inter. Chelsea will only sell if their valuation is met.{@siphillipssport} pic.twitter.com/vNR2bWud9F

The easiest reading of Gusto’s Kante comments is sentimental. It should not be.

For Alonso, who officially begins work on July 1, the important detail is what those comments say about the player Chelsea have been developing.

Gusto has not always looked completely settled at Stamford Bridge. His best games have carried thrust, speed and recovery power, while his less convincing spells have raised questions about final-ball quality and defensive concentration.

That uncertainty is exactly why the next coach matters.

Alonso’s previous teams have leaned heavily on wide players who can receive under pressure, attack space early and still protect the back line when possession breaks down. Gusto has the athletic base for that job.

The test is whether his decision-making can be refined enough to make him a structural piece rather than a rotation asset.

That is where the Kante reference lands with force. Chelsea do not need Gusto to imitate Kante’s position. They need him to absorb the same professional standard: repeat actions, clean defensive choices, no wasted emotion and total commitment to the collective rhythm.

The Marco Palestra deal changes the right side of Chelsea’s squad immediately. Read Chelsea has already examined how Palestra’s arrival offers a tactical clue over Alonso’s defensive plans, and that pressure is now sharpened by market noise around Gusto.

Chelsea can frame the situation in two ways.

One is profit-led. If a club comes close to the reported £75million level, selling a player signed from Lyon for a much lower fee becomes a powerful PSR lever.

The other is football-led. If Alonso sees Gusto as a high-upside wing-back who fits his model, selling him just as the coaching environment becomes more suitable would carry long-term risk.

The complication is Reece James. The captain’s fitness issues, including his current England hamstring concern, mean Chelsea still need genuine right-sided depth.

Palestra may be part of the future, but Gusto is already part of the present. Chelsea have too often needed emergency solutions in that area to treat proven depth lightly.

That is why Read Chelsea’s recent look at James awaiting hamstring scan results matters in this debate. Right-back remains one of the most talented areas of the squad, but also one of the least straightforward.

This is why Gusto’s France rhythm is more valuable than a simple tournament diary entry. Chelsea are watching a player operate inside an elite international camp, learn from a club legend and handle a public transfer valuation that can change his status overnight.

Alonso’s first decision does not have to be final, but it does have to be clear.

If Gusto stays, he needs a defined role early in pre-season and a pathway that makes sense alongside Palestra and James. If Chelsea sell, they need to do it at a number that justifies weakening one of the few squad areas where youth, speed and top-level experience already overlap.

Gusto’s Kante lesson, then, is not just a feel-good Chelsea connection. It is a reminder of the standard Alonso must demand before deciding whether £75million is a price to accept or a warning sign that Chelsea should hold firm.

#2099. Senegal Knockout Tie Gives Chelsea A Double Xabi Alonso Audit

Chelsea’s World Cup watch has moved beyond simple minutes management.

Senegal’s Round-of-32 meeting with Belgium now gives Xabi Alonso a live read on two very different development tracks.

Nicolas Jackson’s attacking authority is the immediate question. Mamadou Sarr’s defensive readiness is the longer-term one.

Chelsea confirmed that Senegal kept their knockout hopes alive with a 5-0 win over Iraq, with Jackson featuring from the bench.

Senegal have since moved into the knockouts, where they face Belgium in Seattle on Wednesday 1 July.

Jackson and Sarr travel into that game with contrasting club questions attached. Both still carry clear Chelsea relevance.

Sarr has not been the loudest Chelsea story of the tournament.

That may actually make this test more useful.

The 20-year-old is still moving through the awkward stage between prospect, squad option and saleable BlueCo asset.

Knockout football against Belgium can sharpen that picture quickly.

The timing is awkward in the best possible way for Chelsea. Alonso steps into a squad with expensive centre-backs, academy-trained options and a board still comfortable using the market aggressively.

A disciplined Sarr performance would not end that search. It would give the recruitment team a stronger internal benchmark before committing another major fee.

Chelsea need defenders who can hold a high line, cover wide channels and still pass through pressure. Belgium should ask those questions more directly than most group-stage opponents.

The context matters. Chelsea’s recall note recorded that Sarr returned from Strasbourg after 18 appearances across Ligue 1 and the Conference League.

The club also noted that he had already earned his first Senegal cap.

That is a different profile from an academy player being carried by reputation alone.

There is a statistical base behind the interest too. When Chelsea completed the deal, beIN SPORTS noted that Sarr made 27 Ligue 1 appearances in his first Strasbourg season.

He helped them keep 10 clean sheets. He also produced a team-high 20 blocks and made 25 interceptions.

Those numbers explain why this Belgium game is not just a sentimental World Cup subplot.

Alonso’s Chelsea defence is already crowded with Levi Colwill, Wesley Fofana, Trevoh Chalobah and external targets still orbiting the plan.

ReadChelsea has already looked at why Colwill’s recovery story gives Alonso a defensive test. Sarr’s value is different.

He is still being evaluated as a physical, long-range defensive project.

Jackson’s role changes the editorial weight of the tie.

Chelsea’s striker picture has been messy for long enough. Any high-pressure international performance now carries wider meaning.

ReadChelsea has already framed the forward’s tournament as a live Senegal striker audit for Chelsea.

Belgium should stress both Chelsea questions at once.

Jackson has to offer penalty-box threat and transition security. Sarr, if trusted, has to defend space against a technically cleaner opponent.

He also has to show whether his recovery pace and frame translate under tournament pressure.

Sarr’s own mentality profile is worth noting. In a Chelsea World Cup Q&A, he named Lionel Messi, Sadio Mane and Paul Pogba as his tournament heroes.

His answer on Pogba focused on leadership and group mentality.

That lands neatly with the exact question Chelsea now need answered. Is there enough presence to go with the physical tools?

This is why Belgium feels like a pathway check rather than a routine World Cup watch item.

Chelsea do not need Sarr to solve the defence in July. They need clearer evidence on whether he belongs in Alonso’s first pre-season group.

He could need another loan. He could also become part of the club’s centre-back market arithmetic.

For Jackson, the question is more immediate. For Sarr, it is more structural.

Together, Senegal’s knockout tie gives Chelsea a rare clean look at two players on opposite ends of the same squad-building argument.

Who can help now, and who is worth protecting for later?

#2098. Atletico Madrid eye January move for Arsenal midfielder Martin Zubimendi

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Atletico Madrid are looking to make an ambitious January swoop for Martin Zubimendi after the Arsenal midfielder failed to convince Mikel Arteta to give him a regular starting spot.

The Spain international has started all seven of his Premier League appearances this season, coming off the bench. This has reportedly caught the eye of Diego Simeone, who wants to bolster his midfield in the winter window.

Atletico have marked Zubimendi as a possible target, according to Spanish radio station RNE via SPORT. Sporting director Mateu Alemany feels that there is still more to do with Simeone’s squad despite big investments in new players this summer.

If Zubimendi’s situation fails to improve at Arsenal, he could become one of the most wanted signings of January.

Zubimendi played a major role in Arsenal’s Premier League title-winning campaign last season. He made 34 league starts, scoring five goals and providing one assist.

He played a key role in the club’s run to the Champions League final. Since then Arsenal’s midfield has changed.

Injuries have hampered his campaign, but Myles Lewis-Skelly has returned to the midfield, and Bruno Guimaraes joined in the summer.

The 27-year-old has also expressed frustration at the lack of playing opportunities available to him. He was signed for £60 million from Real Sociedad to Arsenal just 18 months ago and has three-and-a-half years left on his contract.

Any move to Atletico would be a battle. Real Madrid are also reportedly fans of the midfielder, and he has also been linked with a move to Chelsea in recent months.

Atletico are keen to bring Zubimendi in alongside Pablo Barrios in central midfield.

It would be a dream combination for Simeone, and the Spanish club are willing to fund any move.

Another option could see him involved in a deal for Julian Alvarez. There are currently no talks about a potential swap deal.

Arteta has previously played down concerns about Zubimendi’s importance to Arsenal. Earlier this month, he highlighted the midfielder’s commitment and suggested injuries had affected his opportunities.

The Arsenal manager also made clear that Zubimendi would have chances to play, but would need to take them when they arrived.

That leaves the midfielder with an opportunity to work his way back into Arteta’s plans. However, Atletico’s reported interest could add another dimension to his situation as January approaches.
